Dexter U.S. FoxDot.

A practical manual for the editor, musical patterns, instruments, effect chains, SuperCollider tab, and recording tools.

32 persistent effect references · 80 Pattern method signatures · 245 embedded example files

Works offline. Use the contents menu, example/effect filters, or Ctrl+F. Printing expands visible reference entries; filter the library first for a shorter printout.

01 · Start here

This manual is for Dexter U.S. FoxDot VST3 0.4.201, the Windows x64 live-coding plugin. It combines a bounded FoxDot-style Python API with a local, embedded SuperCollider synthesis engine and a separate SuperCollider-language editing tab.

Two languages, one audio engine. Write FoxDot-style players in the Python tab. Write SuperCollider language in the SuperCollider tab. The bundled headless sclang interprets SC language and sends supported commands to the VST’s embedded engine; it is not a second hardware-audio server.

This is a practical guide to this plugin, not a claim of complete FoxDot, Renardo, Sardine, PbindFx, or SuperCollider server compatibility. Optional packs determine which external instruments and effects are actually available.

  1. Load the complete Windows x64 dexter_sc_tone.vst3 bundle into a compatible VST3 host. Keep host audio processing enabled.
  2. Open its editor and select Python. Start with a low host output level.
  3. Paste this short pattern and press Run. Press Stop before trying a different demonstration.
Clock.bpm = 96
Scale.default = Scale.minor
p1 >> pluck(P[0, 2, 4, 7, 4, 2], dur=0.5, sus=0.4, oct=4, amp=0.18)

You should hear a repeating six-note phrase. dur is rhythmic spacing in beats, sus is note sustain in beats, and amp is the player level. If it is silent, first check host output routing, audio processing, master gain, and the editor status.

Current delay changes: 0.4.201 uses discrete multitap repeats, accepts slow explicit effect tempo, and stops regenerating the two new persistent feedback delays when the player stops. Previously buffered sound can still drain once.

Documentation baseline: the published 0.4.201 test-branch source. HTML examples and API tables are derived from that snapshot. Windows CI success is not a guarantee of every host, routing setup, or sound.

02 · Your first 15 minutes

Goal: build a short melody-and-drums loop, change its scale, add a clearly audible delay, adjust the selected player, and save your work. Stay in the Python tab throughout this tutorial.

Use the matching 0.4.201 bundle. The melody uses a built-in instrument. The drum line needs the x/o/hyphen WAV banks in Portable/Samples. The delay uses the matching DexterFeedbackDelayFX SynthDef generated for the base Windows package; no optional native UGen pack is required. If either dependency is missing, keep the dry melody running and follow the installation section before proceeding.

Minutes 0–3 · Make your first melody

  1. Load the VST, open its editor, and keep the host's audio processing enabled. Turn the host output down to a comfortable level.
  2. Press Stop so an older demonstration does not play underneath this one.
  3. Paste the following into the Python tab and press Run once. Wait for Busy to return to Run.
Clock.bpm = 96
Scale.default = Scale.minor
Root.default.set("C")

p1 >> pluck(P[0, 2, 4, 7, 4, 2],
            dur=0.5, sus=0.35, oct=4, amp=0.16)

Listen for: a short six-note pluck phrase, repeating evenly. At 96 BPM, a 0.5-beat step is about 0.313 seconds. If this is silent, solve host routing/master gain first—do not add effects yet.

Try: change dur from 0.5 to 1, then press Run to hear wider spacing. Return it to 0.5 before the next step. Changing sus changes note length, not rhythmic spacing.

Minutes 3–6 · Add a simple drum part

Add this second player below the melody, leaving p1 in place, then press Run once. If you lack the sample banks, skip this line for now; the melody and delay tutorial can still be completed.

p2 >> play("x-o-", dur=0.5, sample=0, amp=0.12, pan=0.15)

Listen for: kick, closed hat, snare, closed hat underneath the pluck phrase. - selects a hat; it is not a rest. Replace a hyphen with a space if you want silence at that position. The small supplied diagnostic WAVs are test sounds, not a full acoustic drum library.

Minutes 6–8 · Change the musical scale

Right-click your Scale.default = Scale.minor line. Choose dorian from the scale menu, then press Run. The resulting line should read Scale.default = Scale.dorian. If the menu is unavailable in an older editor, type that line manually.

Listen for: a different color in the pitched phrase while the sample drum part stays the same. Scale changes affect degree-based notes, not the drum bank. Return to minor if you prefer it.

Minutes 8–11 · Add one delay, not a whole chain

Replace only the p1 assignment with this version. All the effect arguments belong inside the pluck call. Leave your Clock, Scale, Root, and optional p2 lines in place.

p1 >> pluck(P[0, 2, 4, 7, 4, 2],
            dur=0.5, sus=0.2, oct=4, amp=0.14,
            effect="DexterFeedbackDelayFX",
            fx_mix=0.28, fx_mode=0,
            fx_time=0.3, fx_feedback=0.3)

Listen for: quiet repeats behind the short plucks. To isolate them, temporarily set dur=2. The 0.3-second delay is free-running, not locked to the 96 BPM grid. Keep feedback low so repeats remain easy to distinguish.

Sidebar alternative: drag the Feedback Delay entry onto the p1 assignment or use its context menu. Check the generated call before Run. If direct insertion is awkward, use the one-line effect-arguments copy action and paste within the call rather than after its closing parenthesis.

Minutes 11–13 · Adjust the correct live controls

  1. Click p1 in Players or click its assignment in the code. Confirm p1—not p2—is selected.
  2. Select its Feedback Delay chain entry to expose that insert's controls. Start with fx_mix around 0.2–0.4 and fx_feedback around 0.2–0.4.
  3. Move the mix slider gently. The wet repeats should become more or less prominent. Player level changes the whole player, whereas fx_mix changes the dry/wet blend.
  4. Release the slider and check the scalar value written into the p1 call. Save the source if you want that setting to survive your next edit.

If there are no effect sliders, verify the selected player/insert and the loaded definition. Do not change p2's controls expecting p1's delay to move.

Minutes 13–15 · Save, stop, and reopen

Use Save As and give the script a recognizable name such as my-first-groove.py. Stop playback, reopen that file, and Run it. A saved file is different from merely restoring VST project state; restoring state does not execute code automatically.

Your finished script: use this as a clean comparison if earlier edits became confusing. Omit p2 if the diagnostic WAV banks are not installed.

Clock.bpm = 96
Scale.default = Scale.dorian
Root.default.set("C")

p1 >> pluck(P[0, 2, 4, 7, 4, 2],
            dur=0.5, sus=0.2, oct=4, amp=0.14,
            effect="DexterFeedbackDelayFX",
            fx_mix=0.28, fx_mode=0,
            fx_time=0.3, fx_feedback=0.3)
p2 >> play("x-o-", dur=0.5, sample=0, amp=0.12, pan=0.15)

Stop behavior: use the main Stop button to end this demo. In the new feedback delay, regeneration stops and the already-buffered sound may drain once. To stop one player only, run p1.stop() separately.

These Python tutorial blocks were checked against the embedded API. The listening descriptions are expected behavior, not a claim that your host or speakers have been tested.

03 · Install, update, and keep your files safe

A .vst3 bundle is a directory, not just its inner DLL. Copy the complete bundle. An update that replaces only the plugin binary may leave stale SynthDefs behind.

  1. Close the host completely. Back up the existing bundle and your scripts, samples, and custom definitions.
  2. Install the new base bundle using the location your host scans for VST3 plugins. If you merge an update, replace the matching files together.
  3. Install each optional pack’s modules, compiled SynthDefs, sidecars, and examples as one matching set.
  4. Restart the host and confirm the version shown in the panel. Reopen a bundled example from the updated Portable folder.
dexter_sc_tone.vst3/
  Contents/
    Resources/             # bundled engine/runtime resources
  Portable/
    249-multitap-delay-modes.py
    250-feedback-delay-processing.py
    SynthDefs/             # .scsyndef and matching .controls files
    SCPlugins/             # compatible .scx modules and required DLLs
    Samples/               # user WAV banks and disk-stream sources
    Sidechain/             # separate material, not ordinary note instruments

The tree above shows the portable layout; retain the complete structure from the supplied ZIP. Do not put an entire inner ZIP into a folder and expect the plugin to scan it.

Do not erase existing packs to install a new one. Merge carefully, preserve your own files, and avoid duplicate versions of the same native UGen module.

The examples library in this manual includes scripts from the base source and optional packs. A listed example does not mean its optional dependencies are installed in your copy.

04 · Editor, sidebar, and live sliders

Run evaluates the current script on the script worker. While a request is being processed, the UI shows Busy and does not accept another overlapping Run. Stop is the safe first action when switching demonstrations or recovering from a stuck script request.

Open / Save / Save As: use these to manage scripts. The current script is stored with the VST project state, but restoring that state does not automatically run it. Save an ordinary file as well if you want a reusable example.

Players, Synths, Effects, and Samples

  • Players: select the actual player or its code line to inspect live controls. An effect chain belongs to that player.
  • Synths: categorized instruments, including installed precompiled wrappers. The category is index metadata; files do not need to move into category folders.
  • Effects: native note controls and installed player inserts, grouped by family. Categories/subcategories start collapsed. An informational entry is not proof the module is loaded.
  • Samples: inspect sample banks and open the Samples folder. Sample symbols refer to WAV banks, not pitched SynthDefs.

Insert instruments and effects

Start a line such as p3 >>, then use its right-click menu or drag a catalog entry onto that player line. Catalog actions edit the script; press Run to apply the edited graph. Where insertion is unsuitable, use Copy effect args for a pN call and paste inside the instrument’s parentheses. Never append effect keywords after a completed call.

# Correct: the effect controls are inside the instrument call.
p1 >> pluck(P[0, 3, 7], dur=1, amp=0.16,
            effect="DexterFeedbackDelayFX", fx_mix=0.3,
            fx_time=0.3, fx_feedback=0.4)

What live sliders mean

Instrument controls and effect controls are different. Select the player, then the appropriate insert slot in its chain. A first-slot mix control is not a replacement for the other slots’ controls. Ranges are effect-specific; a shared name such as fx_size does not have one global range.

Scalar slider edits are applied live with smoothing where supported and are written back to the relevant source on release. Check the rewritten source before rerunning. A var() or linvar() is an automation expression, not a fixed scalar default; keep a saved copy before replacing one with a slider value. Controls absent from the selected instrument’s sidecar cannot be invented by the editor.

PShift is per-player, not a global effect. Its slider is shown for a selected player using pshift. Right-click a catalog instrument/effect for its About information.

Master output: 0–250%; 100% is unity gain and 0% is mute. More gain does not repair a very low register or a silent source. Reduce the host output before raising it.

05 · Notes, players, scales, and tempo

There are eight named live players, p1–p8. The >> operator assigns a musical pattern to a player. Reassigning one player need not stop the others.

ControlMeaning / practical use
durTime between events, in beats; use positive durations.
susSustain, in beats. It can exceed dur, so notes overlap within the voice bounds.
octRegister for scale-degree synth calls; not a global MIDI transpose.
amp / panPlayer level / left-right position; pan is −1 to +1.
atk / relEnvelope controls. Custom SynthDef note-envelope values are passed in seconds.
sample / rateSample variant / playback rate for WAV calls; these are not synth-selection controls.
Clock.bpm = 100
Scale.default = Scale.dorian
Root.default.set("C")
p1 >> pluck(P[0, 2, 4, 6], dur=0.5, sus=0.4, oct=4, amp=0.16)
p2 >> sine(P[0, 4], dur=2, sus=1.5, oct=3, amp=0.08, pan=0.2)

# Run separately to stop just p1:
# p1.stop()

Choose a scale

Right-click the Scale.default line in the Python editor to choose a supported scale. The choice changes source code; Run applies it. Scale degrees are not literal MIDI notes. The low-level play([60, 64, 67], ...) form uses MIDI pitches by default; ordinary named synth calls use the FoxDot-style degree/octave path.

Tempo and swing

Clock.bpm accepts a scalar and supported var/linvar tempo expressions. Finite BPM must remain at least 0.001; there is no fixed overall BPM ceiling. Very high tempo can still hit event/CPU limits. This does not mean host-transport sync.

Clock.bpm = var([80, 120], [8, 8])      # stepped changes
# Or choose this instead:
# Clock.bpm = linvar([80, 120], [16, 16])
Clock.swing(amount=0.1)                # 0 disables swing

Swing nudges alternating half-beats and is safety-bounded below half a beat. Its phase starts with the player rather than DAW transport. Do not assume every effect follows Clock.bpm automatically: the multitap insert has a separate fx_bpm parameter, capped at 300.

Bass register: the original FoxDot Bass/JBass graphs divide oscillator frequency by four. Try their demonstrated octave-5 register before concluding the voice is silent. This is different from turning up amp on subsonic notes. For clicks, first isolate the voice dry, lower the level, and use a gentler envelope; rapid retriggers and feedback can obscure the cause.

06 · Patterns and musical transformations

P[...] builds a finite pattern. A list of notes is sequential; a group is simultaneous. Preserve that distinction when transforming nested data.

notes = P[0, 2, 4, 7]
shifted = notes + 2
backwards = notes.reverse()
rotated = notes.rotate(1)
chord = P[0, 2, 4].asGroup()
p1 >> pluck(chord, dur=2, sus=1.6, oct=4, amp=0.14)

Common supported families include reversal/rotation, stuttering/repetition, sorting/deduplication, zip/lacing, selection/compression, conversion, nested transformation, and bounded mutation. The signature appendix below is extracted from the actual finite Pattern class—not from an upstream wish list.

Shared patterns versus copies

shared = P[0, 2, 4]
alias = shared
independent = shared.copy()
shared.append(7)       # alias sees the mutation
independent.extend([9, 11])
p1 >> pluck(shared, dur=0.5, amp=0.14)
p2 >> sine(independent, dur=1, oct=4, amp=0.08)

Mutation happens while the script is evaluated. Do not assume changing a Python list later continuously changes an already compiled audio schedule; rerun/reassign the relevant player. Methods such as append/extend/insert/pop/remove intentionally differ from non-mutating transformations.

Random values and reproducibility

Seed.default = 42
melody = PRand([0, 2, 4, 7], length=16, seed=12)
p1 >> pluck(melody, dur=0.5, amp=0.16)

Seeded finite generators make repeatable choices when evaluated. Their output is bounded; they are not unlimited Python generators running on the audio thread. Useful families include PRand, PWhite, PxRand, PwRand, PBern, P10, PSine, PTri, PSum, and finite ramp. See the embedded example library for their exact call forms.

Euclidean/rhythm tools: PDur, PBeat, PDelay, PStrum, PQuicken, and the bounded PRhythm subset are available. They are not an implementation of Sardine’s separate pattern languages.

07 · var, linvar, Pvar, and modulation

var(values, durations) switches between values; linvar moves linearly between them. Durations are in beats. Use modulation only on parameters that explicitly accept it.

Clock.bpm = 100
p1 >> pluck(P[0, 3, 7], dur=0.5, oct=4,
            amp=var([0.12, 0.2], [4, 4]),
            pan=linvar([-0.5, 0.5], [8, 8]))

Pvar selects between finite phrases over time. This build also supports var/linvar note durations in the supported Pvar path, but phrase expansion and event-count bounds remain in force. Start with the bundled Pvar demonstrations instead of assuming all nested combinations are valid.

Persistent insert fx_* controls accept supported scalar, var, and linvar forms and are normalized against each effect’s range. Control changes are smoothed in the supported playback path; that does not remove every audible consequence of a large delay-time or mode jump.

# Requires the matching Greyhole pack and SynthDef.
p1 >> pluck(P[0, 3, 7], dur=1, amp=0.14,
            effect="Greyhole", fx_mix=0.3,
            fx_delay=linvar([0.2, 0.65], [8, 8]),
            fx_feedback=var([0.35, 0.55], [4, 4]))

Keep mode/index-like controls on sensible discrete values even where the numeric API permits fractional values. Smooth automation is usually more useful for mix, damping, or tone than for constantly changing algorithm mode.

08 · WAV samples, symbols, slices, and loops

play("x-o-") is a sample string. In this setup x is a kick bank, o a snare bank, and - a closed-hat bank. A space is a rest; a hyphen is not. Uppercase letters can select separate banks.

Portable/Samples/x/lower/kick-01.wav
Portable/Samples/o/lower/snare-01.wav
Portable/Samples/_/hyphen/hat-01.wav
Portable/Samples/a/lower/my-sound.wav
Portable/Samples/a/upper/my-other-sound.wav

Files are ordered by filename; sample= chooses a variant and wraps. Replace WAVs or change banks to swap sounds. Reopen the plugin after changing a WAV already cached by that instance. A large third-party sample library is not implied by the diagnostic fixtures.

p1 >> play("x-o-", dur=0.5, sample=0, amp=0.16)
p2 >> play("oxxxoo", dur=2, sus=0.8, sample=0,
           start=linvar([0.05, 0.55], [2, 2]),
           end=linvar([0.55, 0.95], [2, 2]),
           loop=True, amp=0.08, pan=0.25)

start/end are normalized positions with 0 ≤ start < end ≤ 1. loop=True loops within the selected slice, bounded by sus (or dur if sus is omitted). Normal sample playback is not pitch-preserving time stretch.

SyntaxMeaning
[--]Subdivides its contents within one step.
(xo)Laces choices over successive cycles.
{xo}Finite compiled variation, not unlimited runtime randomization.
<x ><- >Adjacent layers with simultaneous positions.
|x1|Selects WAV variant 1 for that hit.
P['x', 'o'] & P['-', ' ']Bounded sample-symbol chord zipping; each layer retains its own rests.

Supported WAV input: mono/stereo PCM 8/16/24/32-bit or float32, 8–192 kHz. This sample-voice path downmixes stereo files to mono before player panning; that differs from the custom stereo SynthDef path. Normal rate magnitude is 0.0625–8; negative rate reverses playback. Each file is limited to 64 MiB; decoded mono cache budget is 128 MiB.

09 · Custom instruments, wrappers, and UGen packs

A UGen is a DSP building block. A compiled SynthDef is a graph built from those blocks. A player wrapper makes that graph usable as a note instrument or insert in this editor. Installing a raw .scx module does not automatically create a playable catalog entry or a bank of sliders.

  1. Put compatible Windows x64 native modules and required dependency DLLs in Portable/SCPlugins.
  2. Put the matching .scsyndef files in Portable/SynthDefs. Filename stem must match the internal name, using a Python-safe identifier.
  3. Add a matching .controls sidecar when explicitly supported controls should have live sliders.
  4. Restart the host. Look for the name in the loaded SynthDefs list and the categorized index.
p1 >> userSynth("CustomPluck", P[0, 3, 7], dur=0.5,
                sus=0.4, oct=4, amp=0.15)

Mono and stereo

The instrument loader accepts one mono or two stereo signals written through the definition’s out control. Mixed-width or wider multichannel outputs are rejected. Stereo instruments retain their center image and use player pan as balance. Effects and vocoders are not ordinary mono note instruments.

Note instruments can expose freq/amp/pan/gate/atk/rel/sustain controls. The plugin owns note cleanup: custom instrument graphs should not independently free their node with doneAction: 2. Use the supplied wrapper conventions rather than copying a standalone-server patch unchanged.

Explicit slider sidecars

# CustomPluck.controls — names must exist in its SynthDef
brightness 0.0 1.0 0.0
sub 0.0 1.0 0.0

Each declaration is name min max default [linear|log]. Up to seven controls, 8 KiB per sidecar. Log ranges must be positive; routing and reserved note controls cannot be overridden. The general loader allows at most 256 definitions, 8 MiB per file and 64 MiB total.

Native binary compatibility matters. Use packs built for this VST’s pinned SuperCollider 3.15/API-7 engine. A module built for a different API is not made compatible by changing its reported API number. Test a trusted module in isolation and retain a backup; a native module can crash the host.

UGen class files may also be needed by sclang to compile SC-language code that names the UGen. A loaded DSP module alone is not a language-class definition. Use each pack’s language-extension installation instructions; do not drop arbitrary classes into the bundle indiscriminately.

Vocoder: LimutVocoder requires separate carrier/modulator buses and is kept under Sidechain material. It is not exposed as a normal one-input player insert. Do not move it into the ordinary SynthDefs scan just to make it appear in the index.

10 · Categorized instrument and effect directory

191 instrument/player/tool entries and 55 effect/control entries, grouped using the same C++ category/subcategory functions as the editor. Expand a family, then look for its subcategory.

This is a reference catalog, not a scan of your computer. It combines the base source, named effect wrappers, the previously prepared 109-instrument SCLOrk pack, and the PortedPlugins instrument pack. It does not install those files. Other separately supplied/custom packs can add names not listed here; no unexamined pack is presented as complete. Some informational effect entries can appear in an editor even when unavailable.

Instrument names are grouped by musical role, not by their physical folder. Precompiled .scsyndef files still belong directly in Portable/SynthDefs; do not make nested Piano/Snare folders there just to match the index.

Sidecar availability is reported from the specific pack examined here. A later custom-slider update installed on your machine can add controls; the manual does not override that metadata or infer slider ranges from sound names.

How to use the directory: search an exact code name, a family such as snare or bass, or a pack such as SCLOrk. The name shown in the sidebar can differ from the code name. Each row separates its call form, musical role, install requirement, and declared custom slider metadata.

Where the main families live

Synths → Drums & Percussion includes instrument-level groups such as Kick, Snare, Hi-hat, Toms, Cymbals, Hand percussion, and Jazz Kit when matching names are loaded. Synths → Bass separates synth, electric, upright, saw, acid, or sub classifications where names match. Synths → Piano includes keyboard- and organ-named portable patches; the built-in keys voice remains under Keys & Mallets. These are the current index rules, not a universal acoustic taxonomy.

Eleven PortedPlugins instruments are playable wrappers; the raw module collection contains more DSP units than the number of playable instrument entries. The ten PortedPlugins insert wrappers are listed under Effects. Ten JazzKit voices share Drums & Percussion → Jazz Kit. Microphone loop is under Synths → Tools → Microphone and loop tools.

Echo / delay

Echo

echo=0.35

Built-in native control

FoxDot echo is 0..64 beats; delay is 0..8 beats; delaytime is seconds (0..4), decay is beats (0..64), echomix is wet level (0..1). This preset adds echo=0.35.

Effects → Delay → Echo

Native player/note control; no named effect= wrapper is needed.

Entry and useRole / index locationRequirements and controls
Lockhart Wavefolder

PortedLockhartWavefolderFX

effect="PortedLockhartWavefolderFX"

PortedPlugins add-on

PortedPlugins Lockhart wavefolder insert. Cell count is fixed at four; bias and pregain are bounded and smoothed, with a safety waveshaper on the wet signal.

Effects → Granular1 reference entries

Cloud / Slicer / Freeze / Live

Entry and useRole / index locationRequirements and controls
Granular FX

DexterGranularFX

effect="DexterGranularFX"

Base / curated stock-UGen definition

Stock-SuperCollider granular insert with four modes: 0=cloud, 1=stepped slicer, 2=captured freeze, 3=live-player granulation. It processes the selected player's signal; it is not an external hardware-input recorder. fx_density is grains/second and the freeze mode holds its local record buffer when fx_freeze reaches 1.

Effects → Granular → Cloud / Slicer / Freeze / Live

Matching stock-UGen effect SynthDef from the base/curated pack; no extra native module.

Category/label extraction ran against the actual C++ catalog header, rather than a separate handwritten classification. Editor catalog rules · SCLOrk upstream collection. Directory call forms illustrate syntax, not an audition of every patch or a guarantee of every parameter on every graph.

11 · Three different ways to apply effects

PathSyntaxPurpose
Native note/player controlslpf=..., pshift=..., chop=...Built-in musical processing. No effect= name is required.
Persistent player inserteffect='Name', fx_mix=...One graph processes the continuing player stream; useful for continuous delays/reverb.
Ordered persistent chaineffects=[('Name', {...}), ...]Up to four inserts, left to right, each with independent controls.
Per-note event effectfx_event=..., fx_event_mix=...Each note chooses a supported graph/chain, with bounded overlapping tails.

The named effect=, effects=, and fx_event interfaces are this plugin’s extensions. They are not a promise of upstream FoxDot or full PbindFx syntax.

One persistent insert

p1 >> pluck(P[0, 3, 7], dur=1, sus=0.35, amp=0.16,
            effect="DexterFeedbackDelayFX",
            fx_mix=0.3, fx_time=0.3, fx_feedback=0.45)

An ordered chain

# Requires the Chorus and Greyhole definitions/modules.
p1 >> pluck(P[0, 3, 7], dur=1, amp=0.14,
    effects=[
        ("Chorus", {"fx_mix": 0.22, "fx_rate": 0.7}),
        ("Greyhole", {"fx_mix": 0.25, "fx_feedback": 0.45})
    ])

Use either effect= or effects=, not both. With effects=, put each effect’s fx_* values in that effect’s dictionary; do not also add standalone fx_* keywords to the outer call. Chain order changes the sound.

Random per-note effects

# Requires both installed effects.
Seed.default = 42
p1 >> pluck(P[0, 3, 7, 10], dur=0.5, sus=0.3, amp=0.12,
            fx_event=PRand(["Chorus", "Greyhole"], length=16, seed=9),
            fx_event_mix=P[0.2, 0.35, 0.15, 0.3])

The event-effect whitelist is Chorus, Flanger, Phaser, Tremolo, JPverb, Greyhole, LimutPingPong, LimutShimmer, LimutGatedVerb, AirwindowsTapeDelay2, and AirwindowsEchojam. Event chains contain at most two inserts. Per-event mix is broadcast to both; their other controls use defaults. This does not expose the full persistent-control dictionary per event.

Event tails receive silence after the note and decay until quiet or a 12-second cap. At most four tails per player are retained; a fifth retires the oldest. Persistent effects have a different lifetime; do not infer that the event-tail cap describes every reverb mode.

12 · Native control quick reference

These bounds follow the bundled editor reference. Not every control is meaningful on every sound, and musical similarity to upstream does not imply identical DSP in every path.

FamilyBounds
Echo / delayecho 0–64 beats; delay 0–8 beats; delaytime 0–4 seconds; decay 0–64 beats; echomix 0–1.
Reverbreverb selects freeverb/freeverb2/gverb; room, mix, damp 0–1; revtime 0.1–10 seconds; spread 0–100.
Filterslpf/hpf/bpf 20–20,000 Hz, or 0 to disable; lpr/hpr/bpr 0.05–1.
Vibratovib 0–100; depth/vibdepth pitch-depth bounds 0–10. Names with fx_ belong to a separate insert path.
Pitch slide / bendslide, slidefrom, bend −1–4; slidedelay and benddelay 0–1 event fraction.
PShiftpshift −24 to +24 semitones; per-player, not global.
Bits / Crushbits 0–24; crush 1–128.
Shape / Driveshape and drive 0–1.
Chop / Spin / Coarsechop 0–32; spin 0–40; coarse 0–32.
Formant / Cut / Blurformant −1 bypass or 0–7; cut 0–1; blur 0.01–8 beats.
FMod / filter LFOfmod −2000 to +2000 Hz; lforate 0–100; lfowidth 0–1.

Bundled editor reference

13 · Hear the delays clearly

Use short, sparse dry notes. Long sustains, many simultaneous players, very short delay times, high feedback, and a high wet amount can make separate repeats sound like reverb.

Multitap: sync, free time, or chance

# Isolated spaced echoes; stop other demos first.
Clock.bpm = 90
p1 >> pluck(P[0, 7, 3, 10], dur=4, sus=0.12, oct=4, amp=0.14,
            effect="DexterMultiTapDelayFX",
            fx_mix=0.45, fx_mode=1, fx_time=0.3,
            fx_taps=3, fx_feedback=0.15, fx_probability=1.0)
  • fx_mode=0: beat-based tap spacing, using the explicit fx_bpm setting.
  • fx_mode=1: spacing in seconds, independent of musical tempo.
  • fx_mode=2: a fresh chance decision per detected hit enables or skips that hit’s spaced taps. It is not the timed feedback-chance mode below.

At very low fx_bpm, beat-based delay requests can exceed the graph’s finite delay storage. Lower fx_time or use free-seconds mode; allowing slow BPM does not provide unlimited memory. fx_bpm=Clock.bpm is explicit wiring at evaluation time, not an unconditional promise to follow every future clock edit.

Feedback processing

DexterFeedbackDelayFX modes are 0 filtered, 1 saturated, 2 pitch-shifted repeats, 3 stereo cross-feedback, and 4 timed chance cross-feedback. In mode 2 the feedback repeats change pitch; the dry note does not.

Clock.bpm = 96
p1 >> pluck(P[0, 3, 7], dur=4, sus=0.15, amp=0.14,
            effect="DexterFeedbackDelayFX",
            fx_mix=0.4, fx_mode=2, fx_time=0.4,
            fx_feedback=0.42, fx_pitch=1.5,
            fx_interval=2.5, fx_probability=0.65)

For timed chance, change fx_mode to 4. fx_interval is fixed real-time seconds, not beats; fx_probability sets the chance of feedback at each interval. A low chance can be temporarily silent. High feedback can build substantial energy: start low.

Stop in 0.4.201: the two new delay inserts stop regenerating and let buffered sound drain once. A remaining echo is not necessarily a stuck note. If a script continuously assigns players, stop that schedule too.

Airwindows naming

These historic wrapper names are not the generic synth vibrato controls. All exposed Airwindows fx_* values below are normalized 0–1. TapeDelay2 uses fx_vib for time, fx_depth for regeneration, fx_lforate for tone, fx_lfowidth for resonance, fx_rq for the wrapper’s rq control, and fx_blur for its blur setting. Echojam uses fx_vib as layout selector, fx_depth as regeneration, fx_lforate as speed, and fx_blur for internal wetness; fx_mix is the outer dry/wet blend.

The complete per-effect min/max/default tables follow. Do not reuse a setting from another effect solely because the keyword matches.

Embedded API control specifications

15 · Granular: cloud, slice, freeze, live

All four modes use DexterGranularFX, not four independent native modules. They are categorized as effects because they process a player’s sound.

ModeWhat to listen forImportant controls
0 · CloudA grain cloud from captured material.density, size, pitch, position
1 · SlicerRhythmic stepped readout.density, size, position
2 · FreezeHolds captured material once freeze is enabled.freeze, position, size
3 · LiveGranulation of the selected player signal.density, size, pitch; position/freeze unused

fx_size must be 0.01–0.25 seconds. A value such as 1.0 is invalid even though fx_size=1 is valid on some reverbs. Let the source fill the capture buffer before freezing; the bundled freeze example recommends at least four beats at 120 BPM. In live mode, “live” means the player stream—not the microphone.

Use the four isolated examples 243–246 in the library to compare modes. Freeze/granular changes can sound different from ordinary synth envelopes; begin with modest mix and amplitude.

16 · Bounded Sardine-style @swim

This is a finite arrangement-expansion feature in the existing Python API, not a second unrestricted live scheduler. It compiles cycles into bounded events; it does not leave a Python while-loop running in the audio callback.

# Example 219: a bounded Sardine-style @swim phrase.
# The editor expands eight cycles into one finite 16-beat arrangement;
# it does not keep a Python loop running in the audio thread.
Clock.bpm = 100
Scale.default = Scale.minor

@swim(cycles=8, period=2)
def groove(p=2, i=0):
    # The sample choice and the rotated bass phrase change each cycle.
    p1 >> play("x-o-", dur=0.5, sample=i % 2, amp=0.16, pan=-0.2)
    p2 >> bass(P[0, 2, 4, 7].rotate(i % 4), dur=0.5, sus=0.42,
               oct=5, amp=0.45, pan=0.2)
    again(groove, p=p, i=i + 1)

i is the cycle iterator. Supported i-based conditions and the bounded every(n, offset=0) gate can choose phrases per cycle. periods=P[...] is the finite period-pattern shorthand; use it instead of period=, not together.

again(...) selects the supported next-cycle timing/iterator form. @die stops the matching finite schedule’s owned players; its function body is not executed as an arbitrary task. A player reassigned by another script is not supposed to be stopped by the old owner.

Sleep is an offset, not a blocking wait

# Example 231: patterned Sardine-style sleep offsets can spill into later cycles.
Clock.bpm = 100
Scale.default = Scale.minor
Root.default.set("C")

@swim(cycles=8, period=1)
def patterned_oversleep(p=1, i=0):
    # The delay pattern is selected by i: 0.5, 1.25, then repeats.
    p1 >> pluck([i % 7], dur=0.25, sus=0.22, oct=4, amp=0.16, pan=-0.2)
    sleep(P[0.5, 1.25])
    # On every other cycle this lands 0.25 beats into the next cycle.
    p2 >> triangle([(i + 3) % 7], dur=0.25, sus=0.22,
                   oct=3, amp=0.10, pan=0.2)
    again(patterned_oversleep, p=p, i=i + 1)

Pattern-valued sleep selects by i. Oversleep can place events after the next cycle starts; expansion sorts the resulting events chronologically. Per-call/total offsets and the final timeline remain bounded to 4096 beats. Sleep patterns contain at most 256 nonnegative literal beat values.

Chance and bar gating: chance/loaf/on can be used in supported player calls and finite swim phrases. They gate a bounded compiled phrase; use Examples 216 and 221 for precise call forms and reproducible seed behavior.

Not included: Sardine’s complete SPL, Ziffers, or Vortex languages, its unrestricted Python scheduling environment, or its D()/N() senders. Do not paste an arbitrary Sardine file and assume it is a drop-in script.

17 · The SuperCollider editing tab

Select the SuperCollider tab for SC language, not Python. On first use, the portable headless sclang helper loads its class library and connects through the local bridge to the embedded VST engine. Keep the host audio callback running. Do not boot a separate hardware server for this workflow.

Python/FoxDot audio and supported SC-tab audio can run together. SC Stop clears the language-owned group rather than FoxDot’s private nodes. You do not need to shut down FoxDot to run the SC tab.

// Example 223 — use the SuperCollider tab and press Run.
// Keep the host's audio processing enabled. Do not boot another SC server.
// The bundled interpreter uses the existing VST engine.
(
Routine({
    SynthDef(\dexterHostBridge223, { |out=0, freq=330, amp=0.10, gate=1|
        var env = EnvGen.kr(Env.asr(0.02, 1, 0.15), gate, doneAction: 2);
        Out.ar(out, SinOsc.ar(freq) * env * amp ! 2);
    }).add;
    s.sync;
    x = Synth(\dexterHostBridge223, [\freq, 330]);
    0.7.wait;
    x.set(\freq, 440);
    0.7.wait;
    x.set(\freq, 550);
    0.7.wait;
    x.set(\gate, 0);
}).play(SystemClock);
)

// You may leave a FoxDot player running in the Python tab.
// SC Stop clears the SC group, not FoxDot's private nodes.
// No shutdown/reboot of FoxDot is required.

This test should play three successive pitches and release. Run the entire parenthesized block. It demonstrates SynthDef.add, s.sync, creation, setting controls, and release in the SC-language path.

What the local bridge supports

The initial command subset includes status, notify, version, sync, clearSched, d_recv, s_new, n_set, n_run, n_free, g_new, g_freeAll, and g_deepFree, subject to private-node and bounded-message protections. This is not a complete external scsynth server implementation.

Buffer/disk/recording/mapping/query commands, arbitrary tree reordering, and external hardware-server control are not part of this bridge subset. A UGen that expects unsupported buffer or bus setup may need more than a compiled module. Language-control scalars must be finite; timed bundles are block-boundary dispatched, not sample-accurate.

Startup is bounded to 20 seconds and can be canceled. If it times out, capture the status/error and lifecycle diagnostics; repeatedly pressing Run does not repair a missing runtime or handshake. A disappearing editor or host freeze is a fault, not expected headless behavior.

Only run trusted SC and Python code. The bounded musical API is not a security sandbox. Native modules and SynthDefs also execute DSP in the host process.

18 · Microphone loops and disk recording

Mic loop: editor controls, up to eight seconds

  1. Route the microphone or audio source to the VST’s stereo input in the host. A Windows microphone permission alone is not host routing.
  2. Click Record mic loop, then perform for up to eight seconds. Click Finish loop to stop earlier.
  3. Click Play loop to hear it, or Stop loop to stop that loop.

Capture is opt-in and held in memory for the plugin session. The looper does not monitor live mic input and does not export a WAV. The editor controls—not a Python recording call—operate this feature. Find its tools entry in the instrument catalog where available; Example 248 walks through the same controls.

Prevent acoustic feedback: use headphones, keep speaker levels low, and do not create a host loop from output back into the recording input. A silent mic loop usually means the input bus was not routed or capture did not receive audio.

Optional DiskIO pack: different functionality

DiskIn/VDiskIn stream files; DiskOut records the plugin’s post-master stereo output. These require the optional DiskIO_UGens.scx and companion sndfile.dll together. They do not extend the SC-language bridge’s command set.

# Separate Python test, only with the DiskIO pack installed.
DiskOut.record(seconds=30)
# Run separately to finish early:
# DiskOut.stop()

DiskOut duration bounds are 0.05–3600 seconds. It writes safely named output under Portable/Recordings without overwriting existing files. DiskIn/VDiskIn use paths relative to Portable/Samples; slots are 1–8, amp 0–2 and pan −1–1. DiskIn has fixed natural rate; VDiskIn supports 0.0625–8.

RecordBuf versus DiskOut: RecordBuf records into a RAM buffer, with memory proportional to duration, channel count, and sample rate; it does not inherently use less memory for long recordings. DiskOut streams to disk using buffers. The mic looper here is a specific eight-second in-memory feature, not a full RecordBuf/overdub workstation.

19 · Troubleshooting the problems you actually see

SymptomWhat to check / do
Busy / script worker busyWait for Run to return, or Stop before changing demos. Do not submit overlapping evaluations. If it never returns, retain the script and status text for diagnosis.
Effect requires matching SynthDefInstall the effect’s matching player-effect .scsyndef and required native module. Restart the host. Check the loaded external SynthDefs list—not just the sidebar label.
Expected 7 / 9 controls, incompatible layoutOld instrument-style and newer persistent-effect wrappers are not interchangeable. Back up first, then replace the named wrapper with the matching version from the pack; do not rename an incompatible binary to silence the check.
fx_size must be 0.01–0.25You selected the granular insert. Use its size in seconds. Reverb fx_size ranges are different. Consult the exact effect’s table.
userSynth not accepted / name missingCheck installed version, the loaded name list, filename/internal-name agreement, and pack dependencies. Merely copying an example does not install its SynthDef.
Vocoder rejected as mono instrumentRestore it to the Sidechain material rather than ordinary SynthDefs; it requires carrier/modulator routing not provided by the one-player insert path.
No slider for an effect in a chainSelect the correct player and chain slot; native note controls differ from persistent insert parameters. Custom instrument sliders require a valid .controls sidecar.
Bass too quiet or not bass-likeCheck oct and original graph frequency division before boosting gain. Isolate dry sound, lower master level, compare an octave-5 Bass/JBass example.
Clicks or audio becomes silentStop, reduce amp/feedback, remove inserts, isolate a single player, and use gentle attack/release. A surviving editor does not prove the DSP stayed healthy. Retain exact octave/control values and module names.
Multitap sounds like reverbUse one short note every few beats, fx_time around 0.3 seconds in free mode, modest feedback, and no other reverb. Increase spacing before increasing wet level.
Delay continues after StopBuffered echoes may drain once in the new delays. Stop any continuing player/schedule too. Freeze or other persistent effects have different semantics.
SC tab loads forever / editor disappearsThis is not expected. Keep the host audio active, verify complete runtime resources, capture status/lifecycle logs, and reopen after closing the host if needed. Do not boot another server as a workaround.
Missing example / duplicate numberCheck full filename and extension. Python and .scd files can share a number; optional packs can contain additional examples. The manual’s searchable library identifies exact filenames.
Raw module listed, no playable effectA .scx filename is not proof of successful registration and is not a playable wrapper. Check ABI/dependencies, SynthDef, sidecar, then index category.

A useful bug report: plugin panel version, host name, exact script/file, full error text, installed optional module names, whether Python/SC/other players were running, and whether Stop restored audio. Avoid including secrets or unrelated files.

20 · Compatibility and safety limits

The compatibility checker is a preflight assistant, not an audio quality guarantee. It checks the embedded API and loaded definitions; a script can still produce subsonic notes, feedback, a quiet stochastic passage, or high CPU. Conversely, an outdated installed binary can reject a method present in a newer example.

AreaBound / limitation
Playersp1–p8; bounded polyphony, normally eight notes per player. Long overlapping tails can consume voices.
Finite pattern expansionTypically 256 values/events/slots and a 4096-beat timeline; individual helpers can impose tighter limits.
Nested structuresMany finite numeric Pattern operations cap nesting at 16; sample/rhythm grammar can have tighter limits.
Persistent insertsAt most four per player. Controls must belong to the selected effect and remain within its range.
Per-note event insertsAt most two inserts per chain; whitelist, tail count, and 12-second tail cap apply.
TempoOverall finite BPM ≥0.001, no fixed overall upper ceiling. The explicit multitap effect tempo is 0.001–300.
Custom SynthDefsOne/two-channel out layouts; up to 256 scanned defs, 8 MiB each, 64 MiB total.
Custom slider sidecarsSeven controls maximum, 8 KiB; explicit finite ranges/defaults.
Mic loopEight-second session-memory capture; no automatic WAV export or live input monitoring.
Host/network integrationDo not assume DAW tempo/transport sync or a complete external SC server.
Language compatibilityFoxDot-inspired bounded API; selected Renardo/Sardine features. No full SPL/Ziffers/Vortex language.

Never disable safety limits merely to make a script pass. Simplify or split an arrangement instead. Use trusted native binaries and keep an untouched installation available when testing packs.

# Rotate and reverse a scale-degree melody.

# FoxDot-style transformations on finite P patterns.

repeated = P[0, None, 7].repeat(2)

15-pattern-arithmetic-operators.pyBase Portable source · Python
# FoxDot-style finite Pattern arithmetic beyond transposition.
Clock.bpm = 104
Scale.default.set("dorian")

scaled = P[0, 1, 2, 3] * 2
divided = P[8, 9] / P[2, 3]
steps = P[0, 2, 4, 7] % 3
squared = P[0, 1, 2, 3] ** 2
rhythm = P[1, 2] / 2

p1 >> saw(scaled, dur=rhythm, oct=5, sus=0.32, amp=0.34, pan=-0.4)
p2 >> pulse(divided, dur=1, oct=4, sus=0.6, amp=0.22, pan=0.35)

23-reverb-algorithms.pyBase Portable source · Python
# FoxDot-compatible FreeVerb plus two optional algorithm extensions.
Clock.bpm = 100
Scale.default.set("minor")

# FoxDot's usual room/mix controls use FreeVerb.
p1 >> saw([0, 2, 4, 7], dur=0.5, room=0.65, mix=0.2, reverb="freeverb")

# FreeVerb2 keeps a separate stereo reverb path.
p2 >> triangle([7, 4, 2, 0], dur=1, room=0.75, mix=0.3, reverb="freeverb2")

# GVerb adds a longer hall-style tail; revtime/spread are extension controls.
p3 >> pulse([0, 4, 7], dur=2, room=0.8, mix=0.25, reverb="gverb",
            revtime=3.5, spread=25)

Source file at the documented commit

24-foxdot-filters.pyBase Portable source · Python
# FoxDot-style resonant filters with band-pass modulation and envelope-driven swell.
Clock.bpm = 100
Scale.default.set("minor")

# Low-pass: lpf is cutoff frequency; lpr is reciprocal-Q resonance.
p1 >> saw([0, 2, 4, 6, 4, 2, 0, None], dur=0.5,
          lpf=linvar([300, 1800], [4, 4]), lpr=var([0.35, 0.7], [4, 4]),
          amp=0.18)

# High-pass: removes low frequencies while preserving the upper harmonics.
p2 >> triangle([7, 5, 4, 2, 0, 2, 4, None], dur=0.5,
               hpf=var([150, 900], [4, 4]), hpr=0.8, swell=1.15,
               pan=-0.35, amp=0.14)

# Band-pass: keeps a resonant band and sweeps its center frequency.
p3 >> pulse([0, 4, 7, 4, 2, 5, 7, None], dur=0.5,
            bpf=linvar([350, 1200], [4, 4]), bpr=0.5, bpnoise=0.18,
            pan=0.35, amp=0.12)

Source file at the documented commit

26-scales-and-chords.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 26: FoxDot-style scales, custom semitone intervals, and note groups.
Clock.bpm = 104

# Scale.default.set accepts a FoxDot scale name or a custom interval list.
Scale.default.set(Scale.dorian)
p1 >> triangle(P[0, 2, 4, 6, 4, 2, 1, 0], dur=0.5, oct=5, amp=0.45)

# Chords are note groups; each degree is resolved through the player's scale.
p2 >> saw(P[(0, 2, 4), None, (1, 3, 5), None], dur=1, oct=4,
          scale=Scale.melodicMinor, amp=0.3, pan=-0.35)

# Custom scales use ascending semitone intervals beginning at zero.
p3 >> pulse(P[0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6], dur=0.5, oct=5,
            scale=[0, 2, 3, 5, 7, 8, 10], amp=0.2, pan=0.35)

Source file at the documented commit

27-root-and-scale-controls.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 27: FoxDot-style global key, scale assignment, and per-player roots.
Clock.bpm = 104
Scale.default = Scale.dorian
Root.default.set("D")

# Uses the default D Dorian scale and key.
p1 >> triangle(P[0, 2, 4, 6], dur=0.5, oct=5, amp=0.35)

# This player uses its own scale and the pitch-class root G.
p2 >> saw(P[(0, 2, 4), None, (1, 3, 5), None], dur=1, oct=4,
          scale=Scale.harmonicMinor, root=7, amp=0.25, pan=-0.35)

# Note names are also accepted as per-player roots.
p3 >> pulse(P[0, 2, 4, 6], dur=0.5, oct=5, scale=Scale.major,
            root="Eb", amp=0.2, pan=0.35)

Source file at the documented commit

28-roman-numeral-chords.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 28: FoxDot's scale-degree triads and seventh-chord vocabulary.
Clock.bpm = 96
Scale.default = Scale.major
Root.default.set("C")

# Roman-numeral chord constants can be played as chord progressions.
p1 >> triangle(P[I, IV, V7, I7], dur=1, oct=4, amp=0.35)

# PGroup builds a custom chord; this player uses its own minor scale and key.
p2 >> saw(PGroup(0, 2, 4, 7), dur=2, oct=5,
          scale=Scale.minor, root="A", amp=0.25, pan=0.3)

29-strummed-chords.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 29: A gentle two-layer FoxDot-style strum, spaced in beats.
Clock.bpm = 84
Scale.default = Scale.major
Root.default.set("C")

# 0.04 beats is about 29 ms per chord tone at 84 BPM.
p1 >> triangle(P[I, V7, IV, I7], dur=2, sus=1.75, oct=3, strum=0.04, amp=0.28, pan=-0.15)

# strum is the spacing between chord tones; the last tone starts 3x later.
# Keep the total spread short enough that sustained repeated pitches do not overlap.
# The brighter layer varies gently from about 14 to 36 ms between notes.
p2 >> sine(P[V7, I7, IV7, V7], dur=2, sus=1.75, oct=4,
          strum=var([0.02, 0.05], 2), amp=0.16, pan=0.2)

Source file at the documented commit

30-player-mute-controls.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 30: mute one player layer without stopping its pattern.
Clock.bpm = 92
Scale.default = Scale.major
Root.default.set("C")

# Run both parts as written. Then remove only the # before p2.mute() and Run;
# p2 keeps its pattern and MIDI notes resume after p2.unmute().
p1 >> pulse(P[0, None, 0, None, 0, None, 0, None], dur=0.5, oct=3, amp=0.28)
p2 >> triangle(P[I, IV, V7, I], dur=2, sus=1.7, oct=4, strum=0.035, amp=0.2)

# Try each command on its own after the two patterns are running:
#p2.mute()

          atk=0.02, rel=0.12, amp=0.055, pan=0.18)

# LFNoise0 is intentionally static-like; leave it very quiet as texture.
p3 >> noise(P[0, None, None, None], dur=0.5, sus=0.12, oct=5,
            atk=0.01, rel=0.06, amp=0.006, pan=0.35)

39-foxdot-native-bass-varsaw.pyBase Portable source · Python
# 39: Native FoxDot envelopes: no Dexter-specific atk/rel overrides.
# bass divides its input frequency by four, so oct=5 keeps the demo out of
# the subsonic range that can sound like clicks on ordinary speakers.
# It uses the source's 20 ms attack then a sus-length linear decay;
# varsaw rises and falls linearly across two halves of sus.
Clock.bpm = 72
Scale.default.set("minor")

p1 >> bass(P[0, 2, 4, 2], dur=1, sus=0.85, oct=5,
          amp=0.65, pan=-0.2, reverb="none")
p2 >> varsaw(P[0, 2, 4, 2], dur=1, sus=0.85, oct=5,
             rate=0.32, amp=0.7, pan=0.2,
             reverb="freeverb", room=0.25, mix=0.18)

Source file at the documented commit

69-composed-star-and-karp.pyBase Portable source · Python
# 69: A small arrangement, not a single four-note synth test.
# p3 is a quiet sine reference: if ONLY it plays, star/karp are still failing.
# Use version 0.4.47 or later for the new-bank first-render fix.
Clock.bpm = 100
Scale.default = Scale.minor
Root.default.set("C")

p4.stop()
p5.stop()
p6.stop()
p7.stop()
p8.stop()

# Concatenate two phrases, rotate the result, then transpose up two degrees.
phrase = (P[0, 2, 4, 7] | P[9, 7, 4, 2]).rotate(2) + 2
p1 >> star(phrase, dur=P[0.5, 0.5, 1, 1], sus=0.45,
           oct=5, amp=0.4, pan=-0.25, reverb="none")

# A slower, reversed response on a separate player.
response = P[0, 4, 7, 4].reverse()
p2 >> karp(response, dur=2, sus=1, oct=4,
           amp=0.65, pan=0.25, reverb="none")

# The familiar oscillator is deliberately quieter than the two new synths.
p3 >> sine(P[0, 4, 7, 4], dur=2, sus=1.5, oct=4,
           amp=0.08, pan=0, reverb="none")

# After testing, comment out p3's assignment above to remove the reference.

74-nested-play-strings.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 74: FoxDot parentheses lace a group across repetitions; square
# brackets subdivide their contents into one step's time span.
Clock.bpm = 108

# The two short hits fit between the x and o on each line.
p1 >> play("x[--]o", dur=1, amp=0.34, pan=-0.15)

# (a ) plays a then a rest on alternating pattern cycles.
p2 >> play("(a )-", dur=0.5, sample=var([0, 1], 4),
           rate=var([1, -1], 8), amp=0.22, pan=0.2)

75-random-and-layered-strings.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 75: curly braces choose a varied pattern; adjacent angle groups
# layer their symbols at matching beat positions.
Clock.bpm = 96

# Random choice is compiled as an eight-cycle pattern when Run is pressed.
p1 >> play("x{-o[--]}", dur=0.5, amp=0.3)

# x and - sound together at the start of each four-step group.
p2 >> play("<x   ><-   >", dur=0.5, amp=0.16, pan=0.15)

# Identical symbols may be layered; the engine assigns distinct MIDI note
# outputs for simultaneous sample voices while both still play the x sample.
p3 >> play("<x   ><x   >", dur=0.5, amp=0.07, pan=-0.15)

Source file at the documented commit

# Example 83: pulse-string and Euclidean rhythm helpers control sample timing.

85-pquicken-prhythm.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 85: PRhythm turns Euclidean tuples into grouped note delays;
# PQuicken creates a rapidly tightening burst of echoes.
Clock.bpm = 96
Scale.default = Scale.major

# The odd hits expand into the three pulses of a 3-in-8 Euclidean group.
p1 >> play(
    "xoxoxoxo",
    dur=PRhythm([1, (3, 8)]),
    sample=0,
    amp=0.22,
)

# PQuicken returns four bounded beat offsets for each note.
p2 >> sine(
    P[0, 2, 4, 7],
    dur=1,
    sus=0.02,
    delay=PQuicken(0.35, stepsize=2, steps=4),
    oct=4,
    amp=0.16,
    pan=0.2,
)

86-pzip-pzip2-chords.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 86: PZip builds chord patterns; PZip2 filters pairs with a rule.
Clock.bpm = 92
Scale.default = Scale.dorian

# Zip two scale-degree phrases into changing two-note chords.
p1 >> saw(
    PZip(P[0, 2, 4, 5], P[4, 5, 7, 9]),
    dur=2,
    sus=1.6,
    oct=4,
    amp=0.18,
)

# Keep only moving intervals, then play the resulting chord sequence.
p2 >> triangle(
    PZip2(P[0, 1, 2, 3], P[0, 2, 4], rule=lambda a, b: a != b),
    dur=0.5,
    sus=0.42,
    oct=5,
    amp=0.12,
    pan=0.25,
)

Source file at the documented commit

87-overlapping-sustain-voices.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 87: FoxDot allows notes to overlap when sus is longer than dur.
Clock.bpm = 100
Scale.default = Scale.minor

# Repeated pitches ring over one another; the engine keeps a bounded
# eight-voice safety limit per player.
p1 >> sine(P[0, 0, 2, 2], dur=0.5, sus=1.0, oct=4, amp=0.16)

# Sustained chords cross the pattern loop boundary and remain independent voices.
p2 >> triangle(
    P[(0, 2, 4), (2, 4, 6)],
    dur=1,
    sus=1.5,
    oct=5,
    amp=0.12,
    pan=0.2,
)

88-numeric-pattern-transforms.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 88: the pattern transforms also accept numeric note patterns.
# Scale degrees are transformed before the synth plays them.
Clock.bpm = 96
Scale.default = Scale.minor

alternate = PAlt(P[0, 2, 4, 7], P[7, 5, 3, 2])
expanded = PStretch(alternate, 12)
stuttered = PStutter(P[0, 2, 4, 5], P[1, 2])
joined = PJoin([P[0, 4, 7], P[2, 5, 9]])
shuffled = PShuf(P[0, 2, 4, 7, 9, 7])

p1 >> saw(expanded, dur=0.5, sus=0.42, oct=4, amp=0.14)
p2 >> triangle(stuttered, dur=0.25, sus=0.20, oct=5, amp=0.10)
p3 >> sine(PJoin([joined, shuffled]), dur=1, sus=0.82, oct=3, amp=0.09)

91-sample-symbol-chord-zip.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 91: FoxDot's & zips finite sample patterns into simultaneous hits.
# Inputs cycle to their least-common-multiple length (up to 256 steps).
# A space rests only that layer; each zipped hit is limited to 8 symbols.

Clock.bpm = 96

# The kick and snare patterns overlap on the first hit.
p1 >> play(
    P["x", " ", "x", " "] & P["o", "o", " ", "o"],
    dur=0.5,
    sample=0,
    amp=0.24,
)

# Unequal pattern lengths cycle to 6 steps; the two patterns combine into
# simultaneous kick/hat hits wherever both lanes are active.
p2 >> play(
    P["x", " "] & P["-", "o", " "],
    dur=1,
    sample=P[0, 1],
    amp=0.12,
    pan=0.25,
)

Source file at the documented commit

92-sample-pzip-pzip2.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 92: PZip and PZip2 can combine finite sample-symbol patterns.
# Inputs cycle to their least-common-multiple length; each hit is at most 8 symbols.

Clock.bpm = 96

# Three unequal motifs lace across 12 steps. A space silences only its own lane.
p1 >> play(
    PZip(
        P["x", " ", "x"],
        P["o", "o"],
        P["-", " ", "-", "-"],
    ),
    dur=0.5,
    sample=0,
    amp=0.22,
)

# PZip2 filters paired symbols before creating each simultaneous hit.
p2 >> play(
    PZip2(
        P["-", " ", "-", "o"],
        P["o", "x", " ", "-"],
        rule=lambda a, b: a != b,
    ),
    dur=1,
    sample=0,
    amp=0.12,
    pan=0.25,
)

Source file at the documented commit

93-clock-swing.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 93: Clock.swing adds FoxDot's half-beat clock nudge.
# A positive amount pulls events in every other half-beat slightly earlier.
Clock.bpm = 120
Clock.swing(0.04)

# At 120 BPM this is a 40 ms nudge (0.08 beats). Set swing to 0 to disable.
p1 >> triangle([0, 2, 4, 2], dur=0.5, sus=0.32, oct=4, amp=0.28)
p2 >> saw([7, 5, 7, 3], dur=0.5, sus=0.28, oct=3, amp=0.18)

# Clock.swing(0)

Source file at the documented commit

94-user-synthdef-loader.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 94: play a precompiled SynthDef from Portable/SynthDefs.
# Select either player in Players to adjust CustomPluck's brightness/sub sliders.
# Defaults preserve the original sine-only sound. To edit the patch, compile
# its .scd source offline with sclang and restart the DAW.
tempo(112)
Scale.default.set("minor")

p1 >> userSynth("CustomPluck", P[0, 2, 4, 7, 4, 2], dur=0.5, sus=0.42,

99-echojam-drum-delay-demo.pyAirwindows optional pack · Python
# Example 99: a sparse kick/snare-like loop leaves space for Echojam repeats.
# This is synthesized (no drum samples required). The short dry noise ticks
# provide a timing reference while the pitched hits run through persistent Echojam.
# Raise depth for longer feedback. fx_mix is the smoothed outer dry/wet amount;
# blur remains Echojam's internal color/mix control.
Clock.bpm = 96
Scale.default.set("minor")

# Low kick-like hit on beat 1; higher snare-like hit on beat 3.
# Rests and short sustain make the repeats easier to hear between hits.
p1 >> userSynth(
    "AirwindowsEchojam", P[0, None, None, None, 7, None, None, None],
    dur=0.5, sus=0.10, oct=2, amp=0.20, pan=-0.12, atk=0.005, rel=0.16,
    vib=0.29, depth=0.62, lforate=0.48, fx_mix=0.72, blur=0.82
)

# Dry synthetic hi-hat ticks, deliberately not sent through the delay.
p2 >> noise(
    P[0, None], dur=0.5, sus=0.04, oct=6, amp=0.035, pan=0.16,
    atk=0.003, rel=0.025
)

100-sample-loop-and-slicing.pyBase Portable source · Python

          amp=0.30, pan=-0.25)

# sus is omitted), so the loop cannot leave a voice running indefinitely.

105-airwindows-timevar-controls.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 105: FoxDot-style var()/linvar() motion on persistent Airwindows inserts.
# Install both Airwindows SynthDefs and the matching UGen pack first.
Clock.bpm = 100

# TapeDelay2's step controls glide briefly at each var() change; linvar() sweeps
# continuously with the player's beat clock. Every fx_* value stays in 0..1.
p1 >> play("x---x---", dur=0.5, sample=0,
          effect="AirwindowsTapeDelay2",
          fx_vib=linvar([0.18, 0.72], [4, 4]),
          fx_depth=var([0.22, 0.68], [2, 2]),
          fx_lforate=linvar([0.12, 0.58], [4, 4]),
          fx_lfowidth=var([0.12, 0.48], [4, 4]),
          fx_rq=linvar([0.14, 0.52], [4, 4]),
          fx_blur=var([0.22, 0.70], [4, 4]),
          amp=0.22)

# Echojam can move independently; it exposes vib, depth, lforate and blur.
p2 >> play("--o---o-", dur=0.5, sample=0,
          effect="AirwindowsEchojam",
          fx_vib=var([0.20, 0.66], [3, 3]),
          fx_depth=linvar([0.18, 0.62], [3, 3]),
          fx_lforate=var([0.16, 0.54], [3, 3]),
          fx_blur=linvar([0.24, 0.74], [3, 3]),
          amp=0.16, pan=0.25)

# A dry reference makes it easier to hear what the two inserts are changing.
p3 >> play("----x---", dur=0.5, sample=0, amp=0.10, pan=-0.25)

# Example 106 — optional DiskIO pack, VST 0.4.80 or newer.

# Run this line on its own to release the file and stream buffer:
# DiskIn.stop(slot=1)
# The plugin's Stop button also stops streams and finalizes any recording.

109-player-degree-follow.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 109: Player degree references keep one part following another.
Clock.bpm = 108
Scale.default.set("dorian")

# Keep this reference line in the editor. When you change p1's degrees and run
# the script again, p2 and p3 pick up the new notes without replacing their
# own synth, rhythm, octave, pan, or level.
p1 >> pluck(P[0, 2, 4, 6, 5, 3, 1, 4], dur=0.5, sus=0.38,
           oct=5, amp=0.24, pan=-0.25)
p2 >> bass(P[0, 0, 4, 0, 3, 0, 5, 0], dur=0.5, sus=0.45,
           oct=5, amp=0.32, pan=0.2)
p3 >> triangle(P[0, None, 2, None, 4, None, 3, None], dur=0.5,
               sus=0.3, oct=5, amp=0.12, pan=0.35)

# Either FoxDot-style form creates a live degree relationship.
p2.follow(p1)
p3.degree = p1.degree

# Try changing p1's degree pattern above, then run again: both followers
# update; their bass/triangle timbre and separate controls stay in place.
# p1 >> pluck(P[7, 5, 3, 1, 0, 2, 4, 6], dur=0.5, sus=0.38,
#            oct=5, amp=0.24, pan=-0.25)

110-every-sometimes-players.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 110: bounded, looping FoxDot-style player degree transformations.
Clock.bpm = 112
Scale.default.set("minor")

# Every four beats, reverse the degree cycle. The player keeps playing while
# the embedded runtime expands a bounded, repeating schedule.
p1 >> pluck(P[0, 2, 4, 6], dur=0.5, sus=0.42, oct=5,
           amp=0.22, pan=-0.25)
p1.every(4, "reverse")

# FoxDot 0.6.7's sometimes() uses PRand(8, 32) / 2 beat intervals.
# Dexter chooses a seeded repeating interval phrase so the schedule stays
# bounded and can run without a Python server after evaluation.
p2 >> triangle(P[0, 4], dur=0.25, sus=0.2, oct=6,
               amp=0.11, pan=0.3)
p2.sometimes("rotate")

# Try reverse/rotate on dense single-note patterns first. This embedded
# schedule subset keeps timing, amp, and synth controls in place.

111-player-schedule-shortcuts.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 111: often/rarely shortcuts, degree shuffle, and scheduled stutter.
Clock.bpm = 110
Scale.default.set("minor")

# FoxDot's often() chooses PRand(1, 8) / 2 beat intervals (0.5–4 beats).
p1 >> pluck(P[0, 2, 4, 6], dur=0.5, sus=0.42, oct=5,
            amp=0.2, pan=-0.3)
p1.often("shuffle")

# rarely() uses PRand(32, 64) / 2 beat intervals (16–32 beats).
p2 >> triangle(P[0, 4], dur=0.25, sus=0.2, oct=4,
               amp=0.17, pan=0.3)
p2.rarely("reverse")

# stutter repeats the current note inside its remaining step duration.
# The third argument bounds each trigger to three total notes.
p3 >> sine(P[0, 2, 4, 6], dur=0.5, sus=0.4, oct=3,
           amp=0.12, pan=0.05)
p3.every(1, "stutter", 3)

# Example 112: scheduled stutter repeats matching drum hits, not rests.

p2.stop()
p3.stop()
p4.stop()
p5.stop()
p6.stop()
p7.stop()
p8.stop()

p1 >> play("x-o-", dur=0.5, sample=0, amp=0.42, pan=0)

# Every beat, stutter the hit that starts on that beat into three even hits.
# The beat-1 snare and the next bar's beat-0 kick are repeated.
p1.every(1, "stutter", 3)

# Example 114: cycle offsets and independent schedule identifiers.

115-player-groups.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 115: FoxDot-style groups share live controls and actions.
Clock.bpm = 96
Scale.default = Scale.minor
Root.default.set("A")

p1 >> bass([0, 2, 4, 3], dur=0.5, sus=0.42, amp=0.55, oct=5)
p2 >> pluck([7, 5, 3, 2], dur=1, sus=0.8, amp=0.42, oct=4, pan=0.25)

# One assignment updates both players; var/linvar control modulation is retained.
low_end = Group(p1, p2)
low_end.amp = 0.22
low_end.cutoff = linvar([800, 2200], [4, 4])
low_end.reverb = "freeverb"
low_end.mix = 0.18

# This bounded schedule is applied independently to each group member.
low_end.every(2, "reverse", cycle=8, ident="shared-phrase")

p3 >> play("x---", dur=1, sample=0, amp=0.4)

# Try these separately while performing:
# low_end.solo()  # mute players outside the group
# low_end.unmute()
# low_end.only()  # stop players outside the group
# low_end.stop()  # stop both members
# Master().amp = 0.3  # shared gain for currently active players

drums.dur = 1
drums.sus = 0.3

120-pattern-shaping-helpers.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 120: three bounded FoxDot pattern-shaping helpers.
Clock.bpm = 100
Scale.default = Scale.dorian
Root.default.set("D")

# Cumulative degrees make a rising phrase; it wraps within this finite pattern.
p1 >> pluck(P[1, 1, 2, 3].accum(), dur=1, oct=5, sus=0.72,
            amp=0.2, pan=-0.2)

# Reverse each two-step block for a compact rhythmic-note rearrangement.
p2 >> triangle(P[0, 2, 4, 6, 1, 3].swap(2), dur=0.5, oct=4, sus=0.38,
               amp=0.13, pan=0.25)

# mirror() reverses the chord order and the note order inside each chord.
p3 >> sine(P[PGroup(0, 2, 4), PGroup(1, 3, 5), PGroup(2, 4, 6)].mirror(),
           dur=2, sus=1.5, oct=5, strum=0.025, amp=0.09, pan=0.4)

121-pattern-method-parity.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 121: FoxDot method forms for stretching, looping, and shuffling.
Clock.bpm = 104
Scale.default = Scale.dorian
Root.default.set("D")

# stretch(size) repeats the source until it reaches the requested item count.
p1 >> pluck(P[0, 2, 4, 7].stretch(8), dur=0.5, oct=5, sus=0.38,
            amp=0.18, pan=-0.35)

# loop(n) appends the complete phrase n times.
p2 >> triangle(P[0, 3, 5].loop(2), dur=0.5, oct=4, sus=0.32,
               amp=0.11, pan=0.35)

# shuffle(n) emits independent shuffled copies; chord groups stay intact.

129-pattern-zip-methods.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 129: zip() makes paired-note chords; deepzip/deeprzip retain nesting.
Clock.bpm = 92
Scale.default = Scale.minor
Root.default.set("C")

               dur=1, oct=3, amp=0.12, pan=-0.25)
p3 >> triangle(P[reversed_pairs[0][0], reversed_pairs[1][0]],
               dur=1, oct=4, amp=0.1, pan=0.25)

136-variable-bpm.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 136: one shared tempo curve drives drums and a melody.
# Listen for 8 beats at 90 BPM, 8 at 135 BPM, then 8 at 105 BPM.
# The 24-beat tempo cycle keeps running across both players' shorter loops.
Clock.bpm = var([90, 135, 105], [8, 8, 8])
Scale.default = Scale.minor
Root.default.set("C")

p1 >> play("x-o-", dur=0.5, sample=0, amp=0.32)
p2 >> pluck(P[0, 2, 4, 7, 4, 2], dur=0.5, sus=0.35,
            oct=4, amp=0.20, pan=0.15)

# For a continuous ramp instead, replace the Clock.bpm line above with:
# Clock.bpm = linvar([80, 150], [16, 16])
# This ramps up for 16 beats, then back down for 16 beats.
#
# Durations are BEATS, not seconds. sus is converted to seconds at the note's
# trigger tempo (as in FoxDot); already-playing WAVs are not time-stretched.
# Run starts the players and tempo curve together. This is the plugin's own
# clock, not DAW transport/tempo synchronization.

137-pvar-phrase-switching.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 137: Pvar changes whole phrases while the lead's note lengths vary.
# Phrase spans must line up with the repeating dur pattern.
Clock.bpm = 108
Scale.default = Scale.minor
Root.default.set("C")

# The eight-note melody switches every six beats and restarts at phrase item 1.
melody_a = P[0, 3, 5, 7, 10, 7, 5, 3]
melody_b = P[0, 5, 7, 10, 12, 10, 7, 5]
p1 >> pluck(Pvar([melody_a, melody_b], [6, 6]),
            dur=P[0.5, 1.0], sus=0.8, oct=4, amp=0.34)

# Spaces are rests; the drum phrase changes on the same six-beat boundary.
p2 >> play(Pvar(["x   o   ", "x x oo o"], [6, 6]),
           dur=0.75, sample=0, amp=0.24, pan=-0.15)

140-numeric-pzip-chord-groups.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 140: PZip/PZip2 combine numeric chord groups into playable chords.
Clock.bpm = 92
Scale.default = Scale.minor

# Each zipped event joins the pitches from both chord patterns. Chord rests
# remain rests; the combined chord is capped at eight notes.
p1 >> pluck(
    PZip(
        P[PGroup(0, 4, 7), PGroup(2, 5, 9), PGroup(4, 7, 11)],
        P[PGroup(12, 16), PGroup(11, 14), PGroup(9, 12)]),
    dur=2, sus=1.6, oct=4, amp=0.24)

# PZip2 also preserves its rule callback, then joins the selected chord groups.
p2 >> triangle(
    PZip2(
        P[PGroup(0, 3), PGroup(2, 5)],
        P[PGroup(7, 11), PGroup(9, 12)],
        rule=lambda left, right: left != right),
    dur=2, sus=1.4, oct=3, amp=0.12, pan=0.25)

141-finite-pattern-generators.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 141: Bounded FoxDot sequence generators.
Clock.bpm = 96
Scale.default = Scale.major

# PRange follows Python/FoxDot range semantics; PSq returns 1**2, 2**2, ...

142-pattern-select-and-compress.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 142: FoxDot-style finite-pattern masks.
Clock.bpm = 96
Scale.default = Scale.major

source = P[0, 2, 4, 5, 7, 9]

156-pattern-insert-pop-remove.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 156: mutate a live phrase while keeping an earlier copy independent.
Clock.bpm = 100
Scale.default = Scale.minor

shared_riff = riff
riff.insert(2, 3)

popped_note = riff.pop()
riff.remove(2)

p1 >> pluck(riff, dur=0.5, oct=4, amp=0.18, pan=-0.2)
p2 >> triangle(shared_riff, dur=0.5, oct=3, amp=0.14, pan=0.2)
p3 >> saw(snapshot, dur=0.5, oct=2, amp=0.12)
p4 >> sine(P[popped_note], dur=1, oct=5, amp=0.08)

161-pattern-comparison-and-inplace-helpers.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 161: comparison masks, cyclic slices, and alias-visible edits.
Clock.bpm = 100
Scale.default = Scale.minor

riff = P[0, 2, 4, 7]
mask = riff.eq(P[0, 2, 5, 7])
selected = riff.select(mask)
ending = riff.getslice(1, 4)

edited = riff.copy()
edited.i_rotate(1)
edited.i_reverse()
edited.i_sort()
edited.i_shuf()
edited.setitem(0, 5)

p1 >> pluck(riff, dur=0.5, oct=4, amp=0.16, pan=-0.25)
p2 >> triangle(selected, dur=1, oct=3, amp=0.12, pan=0.25)
p3 >> sine(ending, dur=0.5, oct=4, amp=0.1)
p4 >> varsaw(edited, dur=0.25, oct=3, amp=0.08, pan=0.4)

164-foxdot-vibrato-slide-bend.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 164: FoxDot vibrato and per-note slide/bend controls.
# vib is the vibrato rate; vibdepth (or the older depth name) is its depth.
# FoxDot's slide/slidefrom/bend SynthDefs apply amplitude envelopes, not pitch
# glides. Each is kept on a separate player here so the contour is easy to hear.
Clock.bpm = 96
Scale.default = Scale.minor
Root.default.set("C")

# Ramps the note level from 1 to 1 + slide during the part after slidedelay.
p1 >> saw([0, 3, 5, 7], dur=1, sus=0.9, slide=P[0.0, 0.55, -0.25, 0.0],
          slidedelay=var([0.35, 0.5], [4, 4]), amp=0.18)

# Starts at 1 + slidefrom, holds for slidedelay, then returns to 1.
p2 >> triangle([7, 5, 3, 0], dur=1, sus=0.9,
               slidefrom=P[0.35, 0.0, -0.2, 0.0],
               slidedelay=0.4, amp=0.15, pan=-0.25)

# Bend rises to its peak after benddelay, then returns to unity by note end.
p3 >> viola([0, 5, 3, 7], dur=1, sus=0.9,
            bend=P[0.25, -0.15, 0.35, 0.0], benddelay=0.2,
            vib=5, vibdepth=var([0.02, 0.04], [4, 4]),
            amp=0.14, pan=0.25)

Source file at the documented commit

165-foxdot-chop-spin.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 165: FoxDot-style chop gates each note; spin moves it across stereo.
Clock.bpm = 120

# Chop divides each note's sustain into repeated half-on, half-quiet pulses.
p1 >> pluck([0, 2, 4, 7], dur=2, sus=2,
            chop=var([4, 8], [4, 4]), amp=0.24)

# Spin follows FoxDot's stereo LFO; its rate is spin/2 Hz.
p2 >> pads([0, 3, 5, 7], dur=4, sus=2,
           spin=linvar([0, 6], [4, 4]), pan=-0.15, amp=0.16)

# Both effects also work on sample players.
p3 >> play("x---", dur=1, sus=1, chop=8, spin=2, amp=0.2, pan=0.2)

166-jpverb-and-greyhole.pySpaceReverbs optional pack · Python
# Optional SpaceReverbs pack; Dexter U.S FoxDot v0.4.134 or newer.
# Both effects run at once, on separate players, and retain their tails
# between notes. These effect= / fx_* keywords are plugin extensions, not
# claims that stock FoxDot has JPverb/Greyhole player keywords.
Clock.bpm = 90
Scale.default = Scale.minor
Root.default.set("C")

# Lush JPverb on a sparse, pitched phrase. fx_t60 is in seconds.
p1 >> pluck(P[0, 2, 4, 7, 4, 2], dur=0.5, sus=0.35, oct=5, amp=0.22,
            effect="JPverb", fx_mix=0.35, fx_t60=4,
            fx_damp=0.4, fx_size=1.2, fx_earlydiff=0.75,
            fx_moddepth=0.08, fx_modfreq=1.5)

# Greyhole makes individual drum echoes easier to hear.
# fx_delay is seconds (not beats); fx_feedback sets the repeat amount.
p2 >> play("x---o---", dur=0.5, amp=0.18,
           effect="Greyhole", fx_mix=0.4, fx_delay=0.5,
           fx_damp=0.25, fx_size=1, fx_diff=0.6,
           fx_feedback=0.72, fx_moddepth=0.06)

# Set fx_mix=0 to hear the dry player, or 1 for effect-only output.

# Example 176: FoxDot-style coarse gating and Formlet formant color.
# coarse=0 leaves a note open; higher values add more pulse cycles per sustain.
# formant accepts 0..7; -1 bypasses the Formlet filter. var/linvar values
# are selected per note so edits stay bounded and live.

Scale.default = Scale.minor
Root.default.set("C")

    amp=0.24,
    pan=-0.25,
)

p2 >> saw(
    [0, 2, 4, 7, 5, 3, 2, 4],
    dur=1,
    sus=0.82,
    formant=var([0, 2, 4, 6], 2),
    amp=0.12,
    pan=0.28,
)

# A sample voice can use the same coarse gate while the pitched line carries
# the formant sweep.
p3 >> play(
    "x---o---",
    sample=0,
    dur=0.5,
    sus=0.45,
    coarse=2,
    amp=0.16,
)

177-pshift-bits-crush.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 177: per-note pitch shift plus a native per-player bitcrusher.
# pshift is in semitones (-24..24) and follows each note.
# bits=0 bypasses quantization; 1..24 lowers bit depth.
# crush=1 is full rate; larger values hold samples for a rougher rate reduction.
Clock.bpm = 100

p1 >> pluck(P[0, 4, 7, 9], dur=0.5, sus=0.45,
            pshift=var([0, 7, 12, -12], 2), amp=0.28)

# The bitcrusher sits on this player's summed output. Both controls are smoothed;
# changing them does not require a SynthDef or an external UGen.
p2 >> saw(P[0, 2, 4, 7], dur=0.25, sus=0.22,
          bits=var([0, 10, 6], 4), crush=var([1, 4, 12], 4),
          amp=0.18, pan=0.2)

# pshift also transposes WAV playback; reverse and very slow rates remain valid.
p3 >> play("x-o-", sample=0, dur=1, sus=0.85,
           pshift=P[0, 12, -12, 7], amp=0.14, pan=-0.25)

# A fixed setting is useful for comparing against the moving version.
p4 >> triangle(P[0, 7, 4, 2], dur=1, sus=0.85,
               pshift=P[0, 12, -12, 7], bits=8, crush=6,
               amp=0.12, pan=0.25)

Source file at the documented commit

183-seeded-random-pattern-helpers.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 183: bounded FoxDot-style index and random-choice generators.
Clock.bpm = 100
Scale.default = Scale.minor
Root.default.set("C")

# PIndex walks through 0..11 once, then the player cycles the finite phrase.
p1 >> pluck(PIndex(12), dur=0.25, amp=0.16)

# PxRand is seeded and never repeats the previous choice.
p2 >> saw(PxRand(P[0, 2, 4, 7, 9], length=32, seed=23),
          dur=0.5, amp=0.14)

# PwRand repeats values in proportion to their integer weights.
p3 >> triangle(PwRand(P[0, 3, 5, 7, 10], P[1, 3, 2, 2, 1],
                     length=32, seed=31),
               dur=0.5, amp=0.15, pan=0.2)

# PxRand can choose sample symbols too; adjacent hits use different symbols.
p4 >> play(PxRand("x-o-", length=32, seed=44),
           sample=0, dur=0.25, amp=0.12)

189-renardo-seed-default.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 189: Renardo's Seed.default controls newly created random patterns.
# Re-running the script with the same default produces the same patterns.

Clock.bpm = 108
Scale.default = Scale.minor
Seed.default = 23

p2 >> triangle(PWhite(0, 7, length=16), dur=0.25,
               oct=3, amp=0.12, pan=0.25)

# An explicit seed overrides Seed.default for just this generator.
p3 >> sine(PRand(0, 12, length=16, seed=99), dur=0.25,
           oct=5, amp=0.08, pan=-0.25)

# Set Seed.default = None to restore normal unseeded randomness for generators

190-renardo-probability-fibonacci-patterns.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 190: two bounded Renardo pattern generators.
Clock.clear()
Clock.bpm = 108
Scale.default = Scale.minor

# PZ12 follows Renardo's documented experimental, deterministic two-token
# deficit ordering. The weights shape the order of these scale degrees.
weighted_notes = PZ12([0, 3], [1.0, 0.45], length=16)
p1 >> pluck(weighted_notes, dur=0.25, oct=4, amp=0.20, pan=-0.2)

# PFibMod supplies the Fibonacci sequence. Modulo keeps the notes inside one
# octave while the player repeats the bounded phrase.
fibonacci_notes = PFibMod(length=16) % 12
p2 >> triangle(fibonacci_notes, dur=0.25, oct=3, amp=0.10, pan=0.2)
p3 >> play("x-o-", dur=0.5, sample=0, amp=0.10)

191-pattern-comparison-operators.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 191: FoxDot-style pattern comparisons create reusable 0/1 masks.
Clock.clear()
Clock.bpm = 104
Scale.default = Scale.major

degrees = P[0, 2, 4, 7, 9, 11]
lower_mask = degrees <= 4
upper_mask = degrees >= 7
same_notes = degrees == P[0, 2, 4, 7, 9, 11]
changed_notes = degrees != P[0, 2, 4, 8, 9, 11]

# select() cycles the mask and keeps the notes whose comparison result is 1.
lower_notes = degrees.select(lower_mask)
upper_notes = degrees.select(upper_mask)

# Unary ~ mirrors both the pattern and any nested group order.
p1 >> pluck(lower_notes, dur=0.5, oct=4, amp=0.20, pan=-0.2)
p2 >> triangle(~upper_notes, dur=0.5, oct=5, amp=0.10, pan=0.25)

# All six relational operators return numeric masks that can be composed.
ascending_mask = degrees < 7
p3 >> sine(degrees.select(ascending_mask), dur=1, oct=3, amp=0.08)

Source file at the documented commit

192-pattern-transform-recursive.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 192: transform applies one safe function to every nested note leaf.
Clock.clear()
Clock.bpm = 100
Scale.default = Scale.minor

source = P[0, PGroup(-2, 4), P[6, 8]]
positive = source.transform(abs)
raised = positive.transform(lambda note: note + 1)

# The nested PGroup stays a simultaneous chord; the nested P stays nested.
p1 >> pluck(raised, dur=1, oct=4, amp=0.18, pan=-0.2)
p2 >> triangle(P[0, -3, PGroup(5, 7)].transform(abs),
               dur=0.5, oct=5, amp=0.10, pan=0.25)

# Example 193: shallow pattern copies and recursive type conversion.
Clock.clear()
Clock.bpm = 96
Scale.default = Scale.dorian

nested = P[2.8, 4.2]
