195-limut-pingpong-duck.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 195: Limut-inspired ping-pong delay and rhythmic ducking.
# Install the optional LimutFX UGen pack first; timing controls use seconds.
Clock.bpm = 120
Scale.default = Scale.minor

# At 120 BPM, 0.5 seconds is one beat. Delay time glides smoothly.
p1 >> pluck(P[0, 3, 7, 10], dur=0.5, oct=4, amp=0.20,
            effect="LimutPingPong", fx_mix=0.32,
            fx_time=var([0.25, 0.375], 4), fx_feedback=0.52)

# The duck envelope drops at each period and recovers over attack seconds.
p2 >> bass(P[0, 0, 7, 3], dur=0.5, oct=5, amp=0.18,
           effect="LimutDuck", fx_mix=1,
           fx_period=0.5, fx_depth=0.68, fx_length=0.07, fx_attack=0.18)

# Both inserts can be ordered on one player; the second insert hears the first.
p3 >> triangle(P[7, 10, 12, 10], dur=1, oct=4, amp=0.10,
               effects=[
                   ("LimutPingPong", {
                       "fx_mix": 0.24, "fx_time": 0.32, "fx_feedback": 0.42}),
                   ("LimutDuck", {
                       "fx_mix": 0.55, "fx_period": 0.5, "fx_depth": 0.55,
                       "fx_length": 0.06, "fx_attack": 0.20})
               ])

196-limut-disperser-fuzz.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 196: a moving all-pass Disperser beside a warm, driven Fuzz.
# Install the optional LimutFX UGen pack first.
Clock.bpm = 110
Scale.default = Scale.minor

# The staged all-pass network shifts transients and phase; it is not a reverb.
p1 >> pluck(P[0, 3, 7, 10], dur=0.5, oct=4, amp=0.22,
            effect="LimutDisperser", fx_mix=0.42,
            fx_frequency=var([420, 760, 1150], 2), fx_q=2.8,
            fx_stages=linvar([8, 20], [4, 4]))

# Drive thickens the harmonics; tone is the post-drive low-pass cutoff in Hz.
p2 >> bass(P[0, 0, 7, 3], dur=1, oct=5, amp=0.20,
           effect="LimutFuzz", fx_mix=0.55,
           fx_drive=var([12, 24], 4), fx_tone=linvar([3500, 9000], [4, 4]))

# Inserts run left-to-right: Disperser first, then a subtler Fuzz stage.
p3 >> triangle(P[7, 10, 12, 10], dur=1, oct=4, amp=0.12,
               effects=[
                   ("LimutDisperser", {
                       "fx_mix": 0.28, "fx_frequency": 900,
                       "fx_q": 2.2, "fx_stages": 14}),
                   ("LimutFuzz", {
                       "fx_mix": 0.24, "fx_drive": 9, "fx_tone": 11000})
               ])

# Example 201: SCLOrk-derived stereo inserts use stock SuperCollider UGens.
# Install the three .scsyndef files from Portable/SynthDefs and restart the DAW.

Scale.default = Scale.minor

           effect="SCLOrkDecimatorFX", fx_mix=0.7, fx_rate=5200,

p3 >> triangle(P[7, 10, 12, 10], dur=2, sus=1.5, oct=4, amp=0.12,
               effect="SCLOrkCrossoverDistortionFX", fx_mix=0.38,
               fx_drive=2.2, fx_shape=0.78, fx_tone=8500)

209-limut-riser-automation.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 209: Limut-inspired one-shot riser automation.
# Each control rises linearly over the requested beat span, then holds its target.
# Restart the transport from beat zero to hear the ramps from their starting values.
Clock.bpm = 96
Scale.default = Scale.minor

# A 16-beat rise in amplitude and filter cutoff.

          amp=riser(16, 0.04, 0.28), cutoff=riser(16, 400, 9000), pan=-0.15)

# A shorter, quieter layer uses the same one-shot control shape.
p2 >> triangle(P[0, 3, 5, 7], dur=1,
               amp=riser(8, 0.02, 0.12), cutoff=riser(8, 700, 6500), pan=0.2)

211-limut-wow-tg-patterns.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 211: seeded slow wow modulation and a fast alternating trigger gate.
Clock.bpm = 96
Scale.default = Scale.minor

# wow() is bounded seeded smooth noise; seed=0 is repeatable by default.
# Center it above zero for amplitude; use the signed default for pan or pitch controls.
p1 >> saw([0, 2, 4, 7], dur=0.5,
          amp=wow(amount=0.18, center=0.32, period=4, seed=7),
          pan=wow(amount=0.65, period=2, seed=9),
          cutoff=wow(amount=1800, center=3600, period=4, seed=13))

# tg() alternates high/low every 1/8 beat; customize the gate depth if needed.
p2 >> play("x-o-", dur=0.25, sample=0,
           amp=tg(0.125, high=0.7, low=0.08), pan=0.2)

212-unit-aware-time-patterns.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 212: explicit seconds, beats, and bar units on existing P/var helpers.
Clock.bpm = 120
Scale.default = Scale.minor

# dur is still measured in beats; seconds() converts each seeded random value.
p1 >> pluck(PRand([0, 2, 4, 7], length=16, seed=21),
           dur=seconds(PWhite(0.25, 0.5, length=16, seed=22)),
           sus=seconds(linvar([0.18, 0.3], [4, 4])),
           amp=0.22)

p2 >> play("x-o-", dur=bars(P[0.25, 0.5]),
           sus=seconds(0.18), sample=0,
           pan=beats(var([-0.25, 0.25], [4, 4])))

215-sardine-span-until.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 215: Sardine-style span stretches a phrase; until limits its repeats.
Clock.bpm = 104
Scale.default = Scale.minor

# A 4-note phrase is 2 beats long; span=1.5 makes each cycle 3 beats.
# It plays six cycles, then stops scheduling new notes.
p1 >> saw(P[0, 2, 4, 7], dur=0.5, sus=0.42, oct=4,
         span=1.5, until=6, amp=0.2)

# The sample phrase is compressed to half its normal cycle and repeats 8 times.
p2 >> play("x o", dur=0.25, sample=0, span=0.5, until=8,
          amp=0.25, pan=0.2)

# Bounds: span is 0.125..8; until is 1..256 cycles.
# Their combined scheduled horizon is capped at 4096 beats.
# Clock.bpm is unchanged; span changes this player's phrase timing only.

Source file at the documented commit

216-sardine-chance-loaf-on.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 216: Sardine-style probability and grouped bar gates.
#
# chance is deterministic per player cycle (and repeatable with Seed.default).
# loaf groups bars; on uses 1-based bar positions within that group.
Clock.bpm = 112
Seed.default = 216

# Play this bass only on bars 1 and 3 of each 4-bar group, with a 75% chance
# on each eligible player cycle.
p1 >> bass([0, 0, 7, 5], dur=1, sus=0.8, oct=5,
          chance="often", loaf=4, on=(1, 3), amp=0.38)

# on without loaf follows Sardine's every-Nth-bar form.
p2 >> play("x---o---", dur=0.5, sample=0, on=2,
           chance="sometimes", amp=0.22, pan=0.2)

# Remove chance= or loaf/on= to hear the uninterrupted pattern.

# Example 218: compare the familiar low kick with the intentionally gritty
# JazzKitBrushKick. Use this instead of Example 217, not at the same time.

219-sardine-swim-finite-cycles.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 219: a bounded Sardine-style @swim phrase.
# The editor expands eight cycles into one finite 16-beat arrangement;
# it does not keep a Python loop running in the audio thread.
Clock.bpm = 100
Scale.default = Scale.minor

@swim(cycles=8, period=2)
def groove(p=2, i=0):
    # The sample choice and the rotated bass phrase change each cycle.
    p1 >> play("x-o-", dur=0.5, sample=i % 2, amp=0.16, pan=-0.2)
    p2 >> bass(P[0, 2, 4, 7].rotate(i % 4), dur=0.5, sus=0.42,
               oct=5, amp=0.45, pan=0.2)
    again(groove, p=p, i=i + 1)

220-sardine-swim-cycle-branches.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 220: bounded @swim branches add a fill every fourth beat.
# The 16 cycles are expanded into one finite arrangement; no live Python
# loop remains running after the pattern is compiled.
Clock.bpm = 108
Scale.default = Scale.minor

@swim(cycles=16, period=1)
def groove(p=1, i=0):
    if i % 4 == 3:
        p1 >> saw(P[0, 2, 4, 7].rotate(i % 4), dur=0.25,
                  sus=0.22, oct=4, amp=0.16, pan=-0.2)
    else:
        p1 >> saw(P[0, 0, 2, 0].rotate(i % 4), dur=0.25,
                  sus=0.22, oct=4, amp=0.16, pan=-0.2)
    p2 >> play("x---", dur=0.25, sample=0, amp=0.18, pan=0.2)
    again(groove, p=p, i=i + 1)

221-sardine-swim-chance-bar-gates.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 221: bounded chance and bar gates inside a Sardine-style @swim.
# The finite cycle expansion keeps these gates in the native player scheduler.
Clock.bpm = 112

@swim(cycles=16, period=1)
def gated_groove(p=1, i=0):
    # Bass only on bars 1 and 3 of each four-bar phrase, with a 50% chance.
    p1 >> bass([0], dur=1, sus=0.8, oct=5, amp=0.3,
               chance=0.5, loaf=4, on=(1, 3))

    # The drums play with Sardine's "often" probability, on every second bar.
    p2 >> play("x---", dur=0.25, sample=0, amp=0.16,
               chance="often", on=2)

    again(gated_groove, p=p, i=i + 1)

222-headless-sclang-remote-engine.scdBase Portable source · SuperCollider
// Example 222 — run from the SuperCollider tab, not the FoxDot/Python tab.
// The variable s is the VST's existing embedded SuperCollider 3.15 server.
// The Run button sends this score to bundled headless sclang; no second
// scsynth/audio server is started.
(
Routine({
    SynthDef(\dexterSclangExample222, { |out=0, freq=220, amp=0.12|
        var env = EnvGen.kr(Env.perc(0.01, 1.4), doneAction: 2);
        var sig = Saw.ar(freq) * amp * env;
        Out.ar(out, sig ! 2);
    }).add;
    s.sync;
    x = Synth(\dexterSclangExample222, [\freq, 220, \amp, 0.12]);
}).play(SystemClock);
)

// This sends a short, self-terminating stereo tone to the VST's main output.
// Stop from the editor's Stop button to cancel routines and free server nodes.

222-sardine-swim-every-cycle.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 222: make a bounded @swim phrase evolve every few cycles.
# every(4) is true on cycle indices 0, 4, 8, 12; offset selects another phase.
Clock.bpm = 108
Scale.default = Scale.minor

@swim(cycles=16, period=1)
def changing_phrase(p=1, i=0):
    if every(4, offset=3):
        p1 >> pluck(P[7, 9, 11, 14], dur=0.25, sus=0.22,
                    oct=4, amp=0.2, pan=-0.15)
    else:
        p1 >> pluck(P[0, 2, 4, 7], dur=0.25, sus=0.22,
                    oct=4, amp=0.2, pan=-0.15)

    again(changing_phrase, p=p, i=i + 1)

223-sardine-swim-variable-cycle-timing.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 223: Sardine-style @swim cycles with a bounded period pattern.
# Periods for successive cycles are 0.5, 1, 0.25, 0.75 beats, then repeat.
Clock.bpm = 108
Scale.default = Scale.minor
Root.default.set("C")

@swim(cycles=8, periods=P[0.5, 1.0, 0.25, 0.75])
def changing_cycle(p=0.5, i=0):
    if i % 4 == 0:
        p1 >> pluck(P[0, 2], dur=0.25, sus=0.22, oct=4, amp=0.2)
    elif i % 4 == 1:
        p1 >> pluck(P[2, 4], dur=0.25, sus=0.22, oct=4, amp=0.2)
    elif i % 4 == 2:
        p1 >> pluck(P[4, 7], dur=0.25, sus=0.22, oct=4, amp=0.2)
    else:
        p1 >> pluck(P[7, 5], dur=0.25, sus=0.22, oct=4, amp=0.2)

    # The decorator selects the next cycle period; dur still spaces the notes.
    again(changing_cycle, i=i + 1)

223-supercollider-host-bridge-check.scdBase Portable source · SuperCollider
// Example 223 — use the SuperCollider tab and press Run.
// Keep the host's audio processing enabled. Do not boot another SC server.
// The bundled interpreter uses the existing VST engine.
(
Routine({
    SynthDef(\dexterHostBridge223, { |out=0, freq=330, amp=0.10, gate=1|
        var env = EnvGen.kr(Env.asr(0.02, 1, 0.15), gate, doneAction: 2);
        Out.ar(out, SinOsc.ar(freq) * env * amp ! 2);
    }).add;
    s.sync;
    x = Synth(\dexterHostBridge223, [\freq, 330]);
    0.7.wait;
    x.set(\freq, 440);
    0.7.wait;
    x.set(\freq, 550);
    0.7.wait;
    x.set(\gate, 0);
}).play(SystemClock);
)

// You may leave a FoxDot player running in the Python tab.
// SC Stop clears the SC group, not FoxDot's private nodes.
// No shutdown/reboot of FoxDot is required.

224-sardine-swim-die-stop.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 224: a finite @swim phrase with a same-name @die stop command.
# Run this file to start the bounded phrase. To stop it before it finishes,
# run the separate snippet at the end of the file.
Clock.bpm = 108
Scale.default = Scale.minor
Root.default.set("C")

@swim(cycles=16, periods=P[0.5, 1.0, 0.25, 0.75])
def stoppable_phrase(p=0.5, i=0):
    if i % 4 == 0:
        p1 >> pluck(P[0, 2], dur=0.25, sus=0.22, oct=4, amp=0.2)
    elif i % 4 == 1:
        p1 >> pluck(P[2, 4], dur=0.25, sus=0.22, oct=4, amp=0.2)
    elif i % 4 == 2:
        p1 >> pluck(P[4, 7], dur=0.25, sus=0.22, oct=4, amp=0.2)
    else:
        p1 >> pluck(P[7, 5], dur=0.25, sus=0.22, oct=4, amp=0.2)

    again(stoppable_phrase, i=i + 1)

# To stop the named bounded phrase while it is playing, run this separately:
# @die
# def stoppable_phrase():
#     pass

225-sardine-swim-sleep-offsets.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 225: bounded beat-accurate sleep offsets inside @swim.
# sleep() advances later events in this cycle; it does not pause Python.
Clock.bpm = 108
Scale.default = Scale.minor
Root.default.set("C")

@swim(cycles=16, period=1)
def call_and_response(p=1, i=0):
    p1 >> pluck([i % 7], dur=0.25, sus=0.22, oct=4, amp=0.19, pan=-0.2)
    sleep(0.5)
    p2 >> triangle([(i % 7) + 3], dur=0.25, sus=0.22,
                   oct=4, amp=0.12, pan=0.2)
    again(call_and_response, p=p, i=i + 1)

# Example 229: seeded per-note Airwindows delay choice and wet amount.

        length=16,
        seed=21,

    amp=0.24,
)

# A dry reference layer makes the random delay changes easier to hear.
p2 >> triangle(P[0, 7, 12, 7], dur=2, sus=1.8, oct=3, amp=0.10, pan=0.25)

231-sardine-swim-patterned-oversleep.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 231: patterned Sardine-style sleep offsets can spill into later cycles.
Clock.bpm = 100
Scale.default = Scale.minor
Root.default.set("C")

@swim(cycles=8, period=1)
def patterned_oversleep(p=1, i=0):
    # The delay pattern is selected by i: 0.5, 1.25, then repeats.
    p1 >> pluck([i % 7], dur=0.25, sus=0.22, oct=4, amp=0.16, pan=-0.2)
    sleep(P[0.5, 1.25])
    # On every other cycle this lands 0.25 beats into the next cycle.
    p2 >> triangle([(i + 3) % 7], dur=0.25, sus=0.22,
                   oct=3, amp=0.10, pan=0.2)
    again(patterned_oversleep, p=p, i=i + 1)

248-mic-loop-demo.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 248: test the editor's stereo mic-loop controls.
#
# 1. Route your host's audio input to the VST's stereo input.
# 2. Run this quiet reference pattern, then click "Record mic loop".
# 3. Play or sing for up to 8 seconds; click "Finish loop".
# 4. Click "Play loop" to hear the captured loop, or "Stop loop" to stop it.
# Recording is explicit, in-memory, and not monitored live or exported to WAV.
Clock.bpm = 88
Scale.default = Scale.minor

p1 >> pluck(P[0, 3, 5, 7], dur=1, oct=4, amp=0.14, pan=-0.25)
p2 >> sine(P[0, 7], dur=2, oct=3, amp=0.08, pan=0.25)

249-multitap-delay-modes.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 249: tempo-synced, free-time, and probabilistic multi-tap delay.
# Deliberately below the old 30 BPM effect floor to exercise slow sync.
Clock.bpm = 24

# fx_mode=0 uses beat subdivisions and follows fx_bpm.
p1 >> pluck(P[0, 3, 7], dur=1, sus=0.8, amp=0.22,
            effect="DexterMultiTapDelayFX", fx_mode=0, fx_time=0.25,
            fx_taps=4, fx_feedback=0.42, fx_probability=0.75,
            fx_bpm=Clock.bpm)

# fx_mode=1 treats fx_time as seconds and always enables the selected taps.
p2 >> play("x---", dur=1, sample=0, amp=0.18,
           effect="DexterMultiTapDelayFX", fx_mode=1, fx_time=0.18,
           fx_taps=3, fx_feedback=0.32, fx_probability=1.0,
           fx_bpm=Clock.bpm)

# fx_mode=2 makes one fresh probability decision per detected hit; when it
# passes, the selected taps play together as a spaced echo pattern.
p3 >> play("o---", dur=1, sample=0, amp=0.15,
           effect="DexterMultiTapDelayFX", fx_mode=2, fx_time=0.25,
           fx_taps=4, fx_feedback=0.38, fx_probability=0.45,
           fx_bpm=Clock.bpm)

Source file at the documented commit

250-feedback-delay-processing.pyBase Portable source · Python
# Example 250: feedback repeats processed inside the delay loop.
Clock.bpm = 96

# Modes: 0 filtered, 1 saturated, 2 pitch-shifted,
# 3 stereo cross-feedback, 4 chance-gated every fx_interval seconds.
p1 >> pluck(P[0, 3, 7, 10], dur=0.5, sus=0.45, amp=0.2,
            effect="DexterFeedbackDelayFX", fx_mode=0, fx_time=0.28,
            fx_feedback=0.58, fx_pitch=1.25, fx_probability=1.0)

p2 >> sine(P[0, 7, 3], dur=1, sus=0.8, amp=0.16,
           effect="DexterFeedbackDelayFX", fx_mode=1, fx_time=0.22,
           fx_feedback=0.46, fx_pitch=1.25, fx_probability=1.0)

# Mode 2 shifts the recirculating repeats; dry notes stay at their original pitch.
p3 >> pluck(P[0, 4, 7], dur=1, sus=0.8, amp=0.14,
            effect="DexterFeedbackDelayFX", fx_mode=2, fx_time=0.3,
            fx_feedback=0.52, fx_pitch=1.5, fx_probability=1.0)

# Mode 4 makes a probability decision every 2.5 real-time seconds.
p4 >> play("x---", dur=1, sample=0, amp=0.12,
           effect="DexterFeedbackDelayFX", fx_mode=4, fx_time=0.2,
           fx_feedback=0.55, fx_pitch=1.25, fx_interval=2.5,
           fx_probability=0.65)

23 · About the author

PROJECT AUTHOR

Dexter U.S.

Dexter U.S.’s music is available on 150 platforms worldwide.

The project’s code is available on GitHub, and the software is available on Patreon.

Dexter U.S. FoxDot brings Python-based live coding, an embedded SuperCollider engine, and a hands-on instrument/effect editor together in a Windows VST3 plugin.

Code on GitHub →   ·   Software on Patreon →

The music/platform and distribution statements and Patreon address above were supplied by the author.

24 · Credits, licenses, and documentation scope

This project incorporates or adapts work from multiple communities. Dexter U.S. project authorship is separate from the authorship of upstream engines, UGens, patterns, synth patches, and libraries.

  • SuperCollider: the synthesis engine, language runtime, standard UGens, and associated documentation.
  • FoxDot: the musical API inspiration and attributed synth/pattern material.
  • Renardo / Sardine / Limut: selected compatibility behavior and musical ideas; not a claim that their full runtimes are bundled.
  • Airwindows, sc3-plugins, PortedPlugins, DaisySP, SCLOrkSynths, and other pack authors: corresponding DSP, wrappers, definitions, or adapted source where included. See each pack’s source/attribution files.
  • CPython and VST3 SDK: runtime and plugin-host infrastructure with their own license terms.

Keep the bundled Licenses/Notices and corresponding source with redistributions where required. GPL/LGPL/share-alike and permissive licenses have different obligations. This manual does not grant new rights to third-party code, certify distribution compliance, or replace the license text.

Generated diagnostic WAV fixtures are documented separately as CC0; do not assume your downloaded sample libraries have the same license.

How this manual was assembled

Effect bounds/defaults were extracted from the embedded Python control specifications. Pattern signatures and example code were extracted from the documented source snapshot. Narrative was checked against the editor/catalog, portable-loader, sample, mic-loop, and delay documentation/code. Older README claims that the product cannot use sclang or custom SynthDefs are superseded by the corresponding current implementation sections.

Published source: 7bdbd2b · version 0.4.201 test branch. This manual is a standalone file; creating it does not alter the installed VST or publish another build.
